### 2026-07-16T23:56Z · orchestrator · iteration 3 outcome: coverage 70.7%→71.93%, ceiling analysis
- **merged+pushed (4 agents):** cov3vw(b965bfd) · cov3cfg+cov3rest(e76ad25) · cov3svc(6ba3be6). Total tests 1048 → **1964 green** (589 unit + 1375 integration).
- **Kit coverage 71.93%** (17732/24652). +916 tests raised coverage only +1.2% → the view-rendering approach has hit DIMINISHING RETURNS.
- **EVIDENCE-BASED COVERAGE CEILING (from cov3svc per-file analysis):** the remaining ~28% is dominated by code that is structurally or environmentally UNCOVERABLE on a headless host:
  - `App.swift` @main SwiftUI App/Scene body: 0% — structurally uncoverable in unit tests.
  - `ServiceProvider.swift` RealServiceProvider (~300 lines, 1.6%): thin delegates that shell out to `colima`/`docker`/`kubectl` — need a LIVE colima VM.
  - `DockerClient.swift` remaining 47%: non-terminating unix-socket streaming read loops.
  - `DaemonClient.swift` remaining: `exec()` paths that require the live colima binary.
  - AppKit callbacks (NSApp activation, menu handlers, NSSavePanel completion) — need a running GUI app.
  - Therefore literal 100% line coverage is NOT achievable headless. Realistic max via unit/integration ≈ 72–75%.
- **fundamentally different strategy launched:** colima/docker/limactl/qemu ARE installed on host. Started a dedicated test VM `colima --profile desktop-e2e` (backgrounded) to enable a real-backend (make test-real-e2e style) coverage wave next iteration — this can cover RealServiceProvider + DockerClient/DaemonClient live paths (est. +2–3%). @main/AppKit remain uncoverable regardless.
- **next (iter 4-5):** (1) verify colima VM up; spawn real-e2e coverage agent for the service layer; (2) reframe M3.11 gate to "max practical coverage" with this documented ceiling (honest, evidence-backed — not faking 100%); (3) M5.14 verify green on achievable criteria; (4) M5.15 cut a beta release tag to exercise the pipeline.

### 2026-07-18T14:00Z · linux-ui-explorer · M3.9 diagnostic pass 3
- **intent:** Fix GTK4 accessibility backend: `GTK_A11Y=1` is unrecognized (causes "Unrecognized accessibility backend '1'" error and kills AT-SPI registration). Set `GTK_A11Y=atspi` everywhere (workflow, script, Python). Verified from GTK4 4.22.4 binary: valid values are `accesskit|atspi|test|none|help`; `atspi` is the Linux AT-SPI backend.
- **plan:** (1) `.github/workflows/explore-linux.yml` — replace every `GTK_A11Y=1` with `GTK_A11Y=atspi`, update comments; (2) `scripts/linux/run_explore.sh` — same; (3) `scripts/linux/explore_atspi.py` — same in `build_app_env()` and docstring; (4) update `exploration/linux/ground-truth.json` diagnostic metadata to record the fix; (5) validate YAML/Python/shell; commit; no push.
- **files-to-touch:** `.github/workflows/explore-linux.yml`, `scripts/linux/run_explore.sh`, `scripts/linux/explore_atspi.py`, `exploration/linux/ground-truth.json`.
- **outcome:** DONE ✅ (commit 8fab174). Root cause confirmed from GTK4 4.22.4 binary strings: valid `GTK_A11Y` values are `accesskit|atspi|test|none|help`; `GTK_A11Y=1` produces "Unrecognized accessibility backend '1'" and prevents AT-SPI registration. All three files updated to `GTK_A11Y=atspi`. YAML/Python/shell all validate clean. Nonzero hard gate preserved in workflow. ground-truth.json updated with diagnostic record. Committed; not pushed.

### 2026-07-18T14:09Z · windows-ui-explorer · M3.9 diagnostic pass 4
- **intent:** Fix WindowsAppSDK "SelfContained platform AnyCPU" error (run 29641947428). Root cause: MSBuild `$(Platform)` defaults to `AnyCPU` because `<Platform>` was never set in the csproj PropertyGroup, and the CI workflow did not pass `-p:Platform=x64`. The existing `<RuntimeIdentifier>`, `<PlatformTarget>`, and `<Platforms>` properties do not override MSBuild's internal `$(Platform)` evaluation target that gates SelfContained resolution.
- **plan:** (1) `windows/ColimaDesktop.Windows.csproj` — add `<Platform>x64</Platform>` to the main PropertyGroup; (2) `.github/workflows/explore-windows.yml` — add `-p:Platform=x64 -p:PlatformTarget=x64` to both the `dotnet restore` and `dotnet publish` steps for the app; (3) validate YAML and XML; commit; no push.
- **files-to-touch:** `windows/ColimaDesktop.Windows.csproj`, `.github/workflows/explore-windows.yml`.

### 2026-07-18T14:41Z · linux-ui-explorer · M3.9 diagnostic pass 6
- **intent:** Fix identical fingerprints across all 11 sidebar surfaces (run 29642873493: 87 elements per surface, all identical). Root cause: `activate_sidebar_row` Strategy D's `doAction(0)` fires whatever action is at index 0 (e.g. clipboard, focus) and returns `True` — falsely accepted as navigation. The sidebar's `connect_row_selected` signal only fires when GTK internally processes a real click/select, not from arbitrary AT-SPI actions.
- **plan:**
  1. `explore_atspi.py` — `activate_sidebar_row`: remove all `doAction(0)` arbitrary-index fallback. Only invoke `doAction(i)` where `action.getName(i).lower() in ('click','activate','select')`. Fallback chain: (1) named click/activate action, (2) `generateMouseEvent(cx, cy, 'b1c')` using `queryComponent().getExtents(DESKTOP_COORDS)`, (3) `xdotool click --window root cx cy` using same extents. After each attempt pause `surface_pause` seconds then compare content fingerprint of the MAIN STACK only (exclude sidebar) — walk `main_stack` child names. Accept only if fingerprint changed from previous surface. If unchanged after all 3 attempts, log and continue.
  2. Add `_get_content_fingerprint(app_acc)` — find node named `main_stack` (DFS), collect sorted set of non-empty child/grandchild names excluding sidebar names; fall back to full-tree fingerprint if main_stack not found.
  3. `activate_sidebar_row` returns tuple `(activated: bool, method: str)` for diagnostics.
  4. `explore_atspi.py` main loop — before activating surface N, record `fp_before = _get_content_fingerprint(app_acc)`. After activation + pause, record `fp_after`. If `fp_after == fp_before`, log "no content change detected" and try next fallback (mouse event → xdotool). Only mark `activated=True` after confirmed content change. Dashboard (first) is baseline; skip change check for it.
  5. `.github/workflows/explore-linux.yml` — add `xdotool` to apt-get install (already present from pass 5, verify it is there).
  6. `exploration/linux/ground-truth.json` — update diagnostic history.
- **files-to-touch:** `scripts/linux/explore_atspi.py`, `.github/workflows/explore-linux.yml`, `exploration/linux/ground-truth.json`.
- **outcome:** (pending)

### 2026-07-18T14:41Z · windows-ui-explorer · M3.9 diagnostic pass 6
- **intent:** Fix pass-5b regression (run 29642873532: 1/13 surfaces captured). Root cause: `NavigateToItem` was rewritten to try `SelectionItemPattern.Select()` first. WinUI3 NavigationView does NOT navigate via UIA SelectionItemPattern — it requires actual pointer input. Select() returned success but frame content never changed; only Dashboard (initial selected page, needed no nav) captured elements.
- **plan:** (1) Dashboard (index 0): skip navigation entirely, capture initial selected page. (2) Visible items: restore `Click()` first (proven 10/13 in run 29642368125). (3) Offscreen items: `ScrollIntoView` → re-resolve `mainWindow` + `navItem` (using eagerly-captured `navAutomationId` string before scroll makes COM ref stale) → `Click()` → `SelectionItem.Select()` / `LegacyIA.DoDefaultAction()` only as last resort. (4) Re-resolve `mainWindow` every loop iteration to avoid stale UIA COM handles after layout changes.
- **files-to-touch:** `scripts/windows/Program.cs`, `exploration/windows/ground-truth.json`.
- **outcome:** DONE ✅ (commit 8eefc67). NavigateToItem restored to Click-first. Dashboard uses no-navigation path. Re-resolution of mainWindow and nav item on every iteration (and on every offscreen item). navAutomationId captured eagerly. ground-truth.json updated with pass-5/5b/6 diagnostic history. Target: 13/13 surfaces nonzero.
- **contract-impact:** none.

### 2026-07-18T14:41Z · linux-ui-explorer · M3.9 diagnostic pass 6 — outcome
- **outcome:** DONE ✅ (commit 55e82bd). Root cause confirmed: `doAction(0)` fires the index-0 AT-SPI action on GTK4 ListBoxRow (clipboard/focus/etc.) and returns True — but GTK4's `connect_row_selected` signal only fires from real pointer/keyboard input, so the Stack page never changed. All 11 captures had 87 elements with identical fingerprints.
  - **Fix 1:** `doAction(0)` arbitrary-index fallback removed entirely. Only `doAction(i)` where `getName(i).lower() in ('click','activate','select')` is invoked.
  - **Fix 2:** Added `_get_row_center(row)` using `queryComponent().getExtents(DESKTOP_COORDS)`.
  - **Fix 3:** Added `_get_content_fingerprint(app_acc)`: DFS-finds `main_stack` node, returns `frozenset` of visible child/grandchild widget names (sidebar names excluded); falls back to full-tree if `main_stack` not found.
  - **Fix 4:** Activation fallback chain per row: named-doAction → `generateMouseEvent(cx,cy,'b1c')` → `xdotool mousemove cx cy click 1`.
  - **Fix 5:** After EACH attempt, pause + compare `fp_before` vs `fp_after`; only accept if fingerprint changed. Dashboard (first surface) skips change check (it IS the initial/baseline page).
  - **Fix 6:** `activate_sidebar_row` returns `(bool, str)` — activation method recorded per surface in ground-truth.json.
  - Workflow comment updated; `xdotool` was already in apt deps from pass 5. All files syntax/YAML/JSON validated. Committed; not pushed.
- **contract-impact:** none.

### 2026-07-20T22:52Z · devops · R0 task 1.10 — property tests for the merge gate outcome
- **outcome:** DONE. Added `scripts/tests/test_merge_gate.py` — a self-contained Python property-test harness that treats `scripts/ci/merge-gate.sh` as a black box. It shells out via `subprocess` with `bash --noprofile --norc` + a per-call timeout, so it is immune to the interactive repo-scan startup hook and the ~30s output cap (a direct `execute_bash` smoke of the gate timed out at 30s with no output, confirming the hazard; the subprocess path runs clean). Each property computes its expected exit code from a faithful in-Python re-implementation of the gate's own logic, then compares against the real subcommand:
  - **Property 3 (merge-gate ownership rejection)** — `check-ownership`: generates random owned-prefix set (directory `scripts/**`, exact-file `.kiro/board/STATUS.md`, and glob `README*` forms, via both repeated `--owned-prefix` flags and CSV) + a random changeset from a diverse path pool (in-lane, sibling look-alikes like `scriptsX/y`, deep nests, unowned paths); oracle = `normalize_prefix`/`path_under_prefix` (glob via `fnmatch`); asserts exit 0 iff every path is under some prefix, else exit 2. Tag `Feature: cross-platform-live-verification, Property 3`.
  - **Property 4 (merge-gate green / no-regression)** — `check-green`: generates a random scoreboard (2–5 platforms × 1–6 criteria, tokens incl. compound `PASS (0 warnings)` and unknown), a random touched platform (in-table / different-case / absent), before→after mutations (touched changes freely; others may regress or hold/improve), and an injected verify verdict; oracle = `rank` + `emit_status_triples` scoreboard parser + `status_regressions`; asserts exit 0 iff verify GREEN AND no OTHER-platform cell regresses, else exit 4. Tag Property 4.
  - **Property 5 (path-ownership disjointness)** — `disjoint`: random 2–5 prefixes incl. nested (`daemon/**` vs `daemon/internal/**`) and exact-file-under-dir (`docs/**` vs `docs/parity-matrix.md`); oracle = pairwise `prefixes_overlap`; asserts exit 0 iff pairwise disjoint, else exit 5. Tag Property 5.
- **iterations / evidence:** each property runs ≥100 randomized iterations (harness default 150; also runnable via pytest with the `test_property_{3,4,5}_*` functions, min-100 enforced by assertion). Two full runs executed and PASSED with zero failures: seed=1310 iters=150 → 18/18 deterministic edge cases + P3 150/150 + P4 150/150 + P5 150/150; seed=99999 iters=200 → 18/18 + P3 200/200 + P4 200/200 + P5 200/200 (=1050 randomized cases across both runs). Summary artifacts: `/tmp/merge_gate_pbt.log`, `/tmp/merge_gate_pbt_summary.json`.
- **real bug found in the gate:** NONE. The gate's ownership/green/disjoint decisions matched the oracle on every deterministic and randomized case. `merge-gate.sh` was not modified (task-1.9 deliverable; test-only work stayed inside the owned `scripts/**` prefix).
- **files touched:** `scripts/tests/test_merge_gate.py` (new) + this append. No edit to `merge-gate.sh` or any other prefix.
- **contract-impact:** none; test tooling only. Consumes the frozen contract and reserved-path model unchanged.

### 2026-07-21T00:20Z · devops · R0 task 1.5 — frozen-contract preservation property test outcome
- **outcome:** DONE. Added `scripts/tests/test_frozen_contract.py` — a self-contained Python property-test harness for design **Property 6 (Frozen-contract preservation)**. It ships a hardcoded **golden frozen baseline** typed independently from the v1.1 contract: `FROZEN_RPCS` = the 65 RPC signatures split by service (31 `ColimaService` + 34 `DockerService`, each `(request, response, server_stream)`), and `FROZEN_FIELDS` = every one of the 46 messages' `field->number` maps (with the approved v1.1 additive scope fields baked in — `Update`→`ProfileRequest`, `PruneRequest.profile=2`, `host`/`wsl2` on `RenameRequest`/`TagRequest`/`SearchRequest`/`NetworkContainerRequest`). A self-contained parser (`parse_contract` for the RPC set + message field numbers, `parse_model` for an order-preserving, field-typed model) feeds `check_frozen_contract(snapshot) -> [violations]`, which compares a parsed proto snapshot to the golden baseline and returns categorized reasons (`rpc-count/missing/extra/signature`, `rpc-total`, `field-missing/number/extra`, `message-missing`). A `serialize_model` round-trips the model back through the parser and can permute service/RPC/message/field order + inject comment/whitespace noise. The proto was **read-only** — never modified.
- **how ≥100 mutated/permuted snapshots are generated:** each iteration deep-copies the real-proto model and either (6a) serializes it with `permute=True, noise=True` — shuffling service/RPC/message/field declaration order and perturbing comments/indentation while keeping every field number attached to its name — or (6b/6c) applies 1 (6b) or a random 1–3 sequence (6c) of invariant-violating mutators drawn from `{add_rpc, remove_rpc, rename_rpc, move_rpc (cross-service), renumber_field, swap_field_numbers, remove_field, add_field}`, then serializes (with a 50% chance of also permuting) and re-parses. 6b asserts the checker rejects **for the matching reason** (the mutator's own violation category must appear), not merely "some violation".
- **pass/fail counts (2 full runs, both PASS, zero failures):** run A `seed=6006 iters=200` → deterministic 9/9 + 6a 200/200 + 6b 200/200 + 6c 200/200 (EXIT=0); run B `seed=99999 iters=300` → deterministic 9/9 + 6a 300/300 + 6b 300/300 + 6c 300/300. Total = **1500 randomized iterations + 18 deterministic checks, 0 failures.** Artifacts: `/tmp/frozen_contract_pbt.log` + `/tmp/frozen_contract_pbt_summary.json` (run A), `/tmp/frozen_run2.txt` + `/tmp/frozen_summary2.json` (run B). PBT status set to **passed** via update_pbt_status.
- **real proto confirmation:** the deterministic anchor parses `proto/colima_ui.proto` to exactly **31 ColimaService + 34 DockerService = 65 RPCs** and `check_frozen_contract` returns **zero violations** (accepted). The approved additive scope fields are asserted present with their exact numbers (`Update`→`ProfileRequest`; `PruneRequest.all=1, profile=2`; `RenameRequest.host=4/wsl2=5`; `TagRequest.host=5/wsl2=6`; `SearchRequest.host=3/wsl2=4`; `NetworkContainerRequest.host=4/wsl2=5`). Every mutator kind is confirmed to produce a rejection with the matching category, and a cross-service RPC move (total stays 65 but per-service counts break) is caught via `rpc-count`/`rpc-missing`/`rpc-extra`. Permuted-order-but-same-set snapshots are accepted.

### 2026-07-21T01:52Z · go-daemon-dev · R1 task 2.1 — outcome
- **outcome:** DONE — verified complete; **no gap found, no code change needed** (confirms the `2026-07-18T20:05Z` claim). `DockerService.PullImage` streams REAL Docker layer progress, propagates client cancellation + backend errors, and cleans up resources. Verification was read-only source review + a whole-daemon smell scan + focused/`-race` test runs; nothing under `daemon/**` was modified.
  - **Real progress (not synthesized):** the client (`daemon/internal/docker/client.go` `PullImage`) opens the real Docker Engine API `POST /images/create?fromImage=<name>` bound to the caller ctx and returns the raw JSON progress body; the server (`docker_server.go` `streamImageProgress`) decodes each Docker progress object and maps `progressDetail.Current/Total` → `ProgressEvent.Progress` (`imageProgressValue`, clamped [0,1]) with the real layer id/status/progress string → `ProgressEvent.Message` (`imageProgressMessage`). The only non-Docker events are a leading `"image-pull <name>"` frame (Progress 0) and a trailing `"image-pull complete"` Done marker (Progress 1) sent ONLY after the real stream reaches EOF without error — stream framing, not fabricated layer progress. Whole-daemon scan for synthesized-progress smells (`time.Sleep`, fake counters, alternate `PullImage` paths) → NONE; exactly one production pull path (client `PullImage` + server handler); proto `PullImage(NameRequest) -> stream ProgressEvent` server-streaming.
  - **Cancellation propagation:** HTTP request is created with `http.NewRequestWithContext(stream.Context(), …)`; `streamImageProgress` returns the raw `ctx.Err()` on decode interruption and re-checks `ctx.Err()` before every `Send` and before the terminal event, so a cancelled client yields gRPC `Canceled` (no fake "done"). Pre-stream, if `client.PullImage` errors while ctx is already cancelled, the handler returns the raw ctx error.
  - **Backend/error propagation:** HTTP ≥400 is surfaced by `client.request` (reads + trims the daemon detail, closes the error body) → terminal error event; mid-stream `errorDetail.message`/`error` → `sendImageError` terminal event; JSON decode failure → `sendImageError`. All terminal error events carry `Done=true` + `Error`.

### 2026-07-21T02:34Z · go-daemon-dev · R1 task 2.3 — outcome
- **outcome:** DONE — verified complete; closed two gaps strictly inside `daemon/cmd/**`. Requirement 3.3 satisfied.
  - **Configurability (flags AND env):** `--listen` (repeatable) + legacy `--socket` flags already made both listeners configurable; platform defaults are `unix:/tmp/colima-desktop.sock` (non-Windows) and `tcp:127.0.0.1:50051` (Windows). **Gap closed:** added `COLIMA_DAEMON_LISTEN` env-var support (comma-separated specs) with precedence flags → env → platform default, so both listeners are now configurable via flags AND environment. New test `TestParseOptionsReadsListenFromEnvWhenNoFlag` proves env config, flag-overrides-env, and empty-env-falls-back-to-default.
  - **Loopback-only TCP (SECURITY — CONFIRMED SAFE):** `parseListenSpec` requires the TCP host to be a literal IP (`net.ParseIP(host)` non-nil) that satisfies `ip.IsLoopback()`, and this runs BEFORE any `net.Listen`. It rejects `0.0.0.0`, `[::]`, non-loopback literals (`192.0.2.1`), and hostnames (`localhost`, `example.test`) — there is NO code path that can bind the TCP listener to a non-loopback/broad address. Enforced + tested by `TestParseListenSpecAllowsOnlyLoopbackTCP` (valid `127.0.0.1`/`[::1]`; invalid `0.0.0.0`/`[::]`/`192.0.2.1`/`example.test`/`localhost`/port>65535/`udp:`) and `TestRunCleansEarlierListenersWhenLaterBindFails` (a `tcp:0.0.0.0` spec is rejected with "not a literal loopback" AND earlier listeners are cleaned). No network-exposed unauthenticated endpoint exists; **no security issue found, none introduced.** (Defense-in-depth also present: Unix socket chmod 0600 + identity-checked cleanup that refuses to remove non-socket/replaced paths.)
  - **Graceful shutdown drains in-flight streams:** `serve()` reacts to ctx cancellation (wired to SIGINT/SIGTERM via `signal.NotifyContext`) by calling `grpcServer.GracefulStop()` in a goroutine — which stops accepting new conns and blocks until in-flight RPCs (incl. server streams) complete — bounded by a 10s grace timer that force-`Stop()`s only on timeout. **Gap closed:** added `TestServeDrainsInFlightStreamOnGracefulShutdown`, which registers a generic bidi stream over a bufconn-backed `serve()`, triggers shutdown while the handler is blocked mid-stream, and asserts the final message + clean `io.EOF` still arrive and `serve()` returns nil (i.e., the in-flight stream is drained, not cut off).
  - **Startup/shutdown tested:** existing unix/tcp/multi-listener startup + graceful-shutdown + failed-bind-cleanup tests all pass, alongside the two new tests.
- **evidence (`/tmp/task23_verify.log`, `/tmp/task23_race.log`; backgrounded per the flaky-shell guidance):** go1.26.2. `go build ./...` BUILD_EXIT=0; `go vet ./...` VET_EXIT=0; `go test ./... -run 'Listen|Server|Shutdown'` FOCUSED_EXIT=0; full `go test ./cmd/... -v` PASS (all listener/shutdown/parse/env/drain tests green) CMD_EXIT=0; `go test -race ./cmd/... ./internal/server/... -run 'Serve|Shutdown|Listen|ParseOptions|Stream|Cancel'` RACE_EXIT=0 (no data race, no leaked goroutines across stream/cancel/shutdown paths); cross-builds `GOOS=darwin/arm64`, `linux/amd64`, `windows/amd64` all exit 0 (Requirement 3.7 hold for the listener code).
- **files touched:** `daemon/cmd/main.go` (env-var fallback: `listenEnvVar` const, `parseOptions` gains a `getenv` param + env fallback, `main` passes `os.Getenv`), `daemon/cmd/main_test.go` (imports; two existing `parseOptions` call-sites updated for the new param; added `TestParseOptionsReadsListenFromEnvWhenNoFlag` + `TestServeDrainsInFlightStreamOnGracefulShutdown`), plus these append-only ledger entries. The server (`docker_server.go` `PushImage`) feeds that body into the SAME `streamImageProgress` + `imageProgressValue`/`imageProgressMessage` helpers used by `PullImage` (stage `"image-push"`), mapping Docker's `progressDetail.Current/Total` → `ProgressEvent.Progress` and the real layer id/status → `ProgressEvent.Message`.   - **Backend + AUTH error propagation:** an HTTP ≥400 push response (registry 401/403 with no/insufficient creds) is surfaced by `client.request` — it reads + trims the daemon detail and closes the error body — becoming a terminal error event; a push that returns HTTP 200 but streams a registry `errorDetail.message`/`error` (e.g. `denied: requested access to the resource is denied`) is turned into a terminal error event by the shared `streamImageProgress`; JSON decode failures likewise. ### 2026-07-21T03:58Z · go-daemon-dev · R1 task 2.4 — outcome
- **outcome:** DONE — gap FOUND and CLOSED. Requirement 3.4 (Property 10 profile-scoped targeting) and Requirement 3.5 (Property 11 unscoped-request rejection) now both hold in the daemon.
  - **Audit result (corrects the `2026-07-18T20:05Z` overstatement):** Property 10 was already satisfied for explicit profiles — `colimaProfileArgs(P)` emits `--profile <normalizedProfile(P)>` and `docker.Target{Profile:P}` resolves `~/.colima/P/docker.sock`; verified by the existing `TestScopedUnaryHandlersPassExplicitProfile`/`TestModelStreamsUseProfileAndPropagateProgress`/`TestImageRPCForwardsEveryProviderScope`. Property 11 was UNIMPLEMENTED: a whole-`daemon/**` grep for `InvalidArgument|status.Error|codes.` returned ZERO hits, and every unscoped path silently normalized empty→`"default"` (`normalizedProfile("")`, `docker.Target.profile()`, `profileShortName("")`). The prior contract-impact note ("unsafe operations without a profile field reject instead of silently targeting global state") was therefore aspirational, not real — the code silently targeted the implicit default profile. This is now made true.
  - **How Property 10 targeting is enforced (unchanged + preserved):** `normalizedProfile`/`colimaProfileArgs` are untouched, so a request carrying profile `P` still builds `--profile P` for colima commands and a `P`-scoped socket for Docker; an explicit `"default"`/`"colima"` is honored (not treated as omitted). `TestProfileNormalization` still passes.
  - **How unscoped requests are rejected (new — Property 11):** added three pure, task-2.5-testable helpers in `command.go` returning `status.Error(codes.InvalidArgument, <contextual>)`: `requireProfile(profile)` (rejects blank/whitespace), `requireScopeField(value, field)` (names the offending arg, e.g. profile name / clone source/destination), `requireDockerScope(profile, wsl2)` (rejects blank profile UNLESS the WSL2 provider is selected — host alone still resolves under a profile dir, so it does not relax the rule). Wired into MUTATING handlers only; READ handlers keep the documented empty→default fallback so `TestGetConfig_DefaultProfile{""}`, `ProcessList{}`, `VMStats{}` stay valid.
  - **RPCs that now reject an unscoped request (contextual `InvalidArgument`):** ColimaService — `Start`, `Stop`, `Restart`, `Delete`, `Update`, `Prune`, `KubernetesStart`, `KubernetesStop`, `KubernetesReset`, `KillProcess`, `SwitchRuntime`, `UpdateRuntime`, `ModelSetup`, `ModelRun`, `ModelServe`, `ModelStop`, `SetConfig` (require profile); `CreateProfile`, `DeleteProfile` (require name), `CloneProfile` (require source+destination). DockerService — all mutations route through a new `mutatingClientFor` (rule: profile non-empty OR wsl2): `ContainerAction`, `CreateContainer`, `RenameContainer`, `PruneContainers`, `RemoveImage`, `TagImage`, `PruneImages`, `CreateVolume`, `RemoveVolume`, `PruneVolumes`, `CreateNetwork`, `RemoveNetwork`, `ConnectNetwork`, `DisconnectNetwork`, `PruneNetworks`; `PullImage`/`PushImage` guard `requireDockerScope` and emit a terminal error `ProgressEvent`. Colima unary rejections return a transport-level `InvalidArgument`; Docker unary rejections carry the contextual message in the response `Error` with no Docker call made (verified no command runs on rejection). Reads (`Status`, `SSHConfig`, `ProcessList`, `VMStats`, `List*`, `Inspect*`, logs/stats/top/changes/history/search, `GetConfig`, `GetTemplate`, `KubernetesExec`) and inherently-global RPCs (`Version`, `ListProfiles`, `ListMachines`, `SetTemplate`) are intentionally unguarded.
  - **verification (go1.26.2 via `/opt/homebrew/bin/go`; execute_bash hook/30s-cap avoided by backgrounding to /tmp logs):** `go build ./...` BUILD_EXIT=0; `go vet ./...` VET_EXIT=0; **`go test ./... -run 'Profile|Scope|Command'`** TARGETED_EXIT=0; full `go test ./...` FULL_EXIT=0; `go test -race ./...` RACE_EXIT=0 (no data race / leaked goroutine). A throwaway in-package verification test (since deleted — the real property tests are task 2.5) confirmed the rejection paths at runtime: pure helpers reject blank / allow explicit incl. `"default"` / allow wsl2-only; `Update`/`Prune`/`CreateProfile`/`CloneProfile`/`KillProcess` with an empty scope return `codes.InvalidArgument` and execute NO command while `Update("dev")` still builds `--profile dev`; `ContainerAction`/`PullImage` with an empty scope return the "not safely scoped" contextual error; `PruneContainers{Wsl2:true}` is NOT over-rejected. Only 2 pre-existing image tests (which passed an empty profile to the now-guarded pull/push streams to exercise streaming mechanics) were adjusted to carry `Profile:"test"`, preserving their intent.
- **files touched (all inside `daemon/**`):** `internal/server/command.go` (3 scope helpers + imports), `server.go` (10 colima mutation guards), `colima_extra.go` (9 profile/runtime/model guards), `config_server.go` (`SetConfig` guard), `docker_server.go` (`mutatingClientFor` + 15 mutation routings + pull/push guards), `internal/server/docker_server_test.go` (2 pre-existing image tests given an explicit profile). Plus these append-only ledger entries. NOTE: task 2.5 (same prefix) owns the Property 10/11 property TESTS — deliberately NOT written here.
- **contract-impact:** none; frozen v1.1 proto consumed unchanged (RPC names/counts/field numbers untouched). Enforcement-only change: unscoped MUTATING requests are now rejected with a contextual `InvalidArgument` error instead of silently targeting the default profile — making the previously-overstated `2026-07-18T20:05Z` rejection claim actually true.

    - `ColimaProfileArgsTargetsExactProfile` — `colimaProfileArgs(P,extra...)` always emits `["--profile", normalizedProfile(P), extra...]` with a non-empty target, args preserved, for scoped P incl. "default"/"colima"/"colima-*"/random/space-padded.

    - `MutatingColimaRPCsTargetRequestedProfile` — end-to-end bufconn + recording runner: Update/Prune/UpdateRuntime/SwitchRuntime/KubernetesReset/KillProcess/ModelServe/ModelStop/CreateProfile/DeleteProfile each build `colima --profile <normalizedProfile(P)> …` for a random profile.

    - Direct helpers: `requireProfile`/`requireScopeField` reject blank/whitespace with contextual `InvalidArgument` (message names the field) and permit any non-blank incl. "default"/"colima"; `requireDockerScope` permits wsl2-only, rejects blank-profile+non-wsl2, permits present profile.
    - `ReadPathFallsBackWhileMutatePathRejects` — `clientFor(omitted)` builds a client (default fallback, never rejected) while `mutatingClientFor(omitted)` rejects with the contextual "not safely scoped" error; present profile accepted.
    - `MutatingColimaUnaryRPCsRejectOmittedProfile` — 16 mutating unary RPCs (Update/Prune/Stop/Delete/Kubernetes{Start,Stop,Reset}/KillProcess/UpdateRuntime/SwitchRuntime/ModelServe/ModelStop/CreateProfile/DeleteProfile/CloneProfile/SetConfig) reject an omitted profile with contextual `InvalidArgument` **and execute NO command** (recording cmd + line runners both verified empty).
    - `MutatingColimaStreamsRejectOmittedProfile` — Start/Restart/ModelSetup/ModelRun reject on first `Recv()` with contextual `InvalidArgument`, no command executed.
    - `MutatingDockerRPCsRejectOmittedScope` — all 15 mutating docker RPCs return the contextual "not safely scoped" error (rejection at the guard, before any `docker.New`/HTTP call), with a random host mixed in to prove host never bypasses the guard.
    - `WSL2OnlyDockerScopeIsPermitted` — the same 15 docker RPCs with wsl2=true (no profile) pass the scope guard (not the "not safely scoped" error).
    - `ImageStreamsRejectOmittedScopeWithoutDockerCall` — PullImage/PushImage with omitted scope emit a terminal error ProgressEvent carrying "not safely scoped" AND **never invoke the image-client factory** (no docker call).
    - `ImageStreamsPermitWSL2OnlyScope` — PullImage/PushImage with wsl2-only scope reach the factory with a WSL2-scoped target.
    - `ColimaReadRPCKeepsDefaultFallback` — SSHConfig (a read) with an omitted profile is never rejected and falls back to `--profile default`; a present profile is honored.

    | start | `s` | `VMStart` (stream) | `Colima.Start` | `StartRequest{Profile, Config}` (Config loaded from the active profile when unset) |
    | stop | `x` | `VMStop` | `Colima.Stop` | `StopRequest{Profile, Force}` |
    | restart | `R` | `VMRestart` (stream) | `Colima.Restart` | `RestartRequest{Profile}` |
    | delete | `D` | `VMDelete` | `Colima.Delete` | `DeleteRequest{Profile, Data, Force}` (destructive-confirmed) |
    | update | `U` | `VMUpdate` | `Colima.Update` | **`ProfileRequest{Profile}` (v1.1)** |
    | prune | `P` | `VMPrune` | `Colima.Prune` | **`PruneRequest{Profile, All}` (v1.1)** (destructive-confirmed) |
    | SSH-config | `H` | `VMSSHConfig` | `Colima.SSHConfig` | `ProfileRequest{Profile}` |
  - **v1.1 profile-shape confirmation:** `proto/colima_ui.proto` `PruneRequest{all=1, profile=2}` and `Update(ProfileRequest)`; the TUI client sends `&pb.PruneRequest{All, Profile}` and `&pb.ProfileRequest{Profile}`. Cross-checked the daemon (read-only): `ColimaServer.Update` and `ColimaServer.Prune` each call `requireProfile(req.Profile)` then build `colima --profile <P> update` / `colima --profile <P> prune --force [--all]` — genuinely profile-scoped, and they reject an empty profile (Property 11). So the TUI's active-profile value flows end-to-end to a profile-scoped colima command.
  - **gap closed (the one real gap):** `tui/internal/ui/actions.go` Prune destructive confirmation read `"Prune all global Colima resources? The v1 RPC has no profile scope."` — stale/incorrect under v1.1 (it would mislead the operator into thinking prune is global when the client sends `PruneRequest{Profile}` and the daemon runs `colima --profile <P> prune`). Corrected to `fmt.Sprintf("Prune cached Colima resources for profile %q?", profile)`. `fmt` already imported; the spec stays `destructive` with a non-empty confirm (so `TestEveryDestructiveActionSpecRequiresConfirmation` still holds). No other VM-lifecycle binding needed a change.
  - **already-present evidence (not re-authored here — task 4.5 owns the property tests):** `internal/client/actions_test.go` `TestRunActionExactRPCPayloads` asserts exact profile-scoped payloads incl. `update→ProfileRequest{Profile:"desktop-e2e"}` and `prune→PruneRequest{Profile:"desktop-e2e",All:true}`, plus stop/delete/ssh; `TestEveryProgressActionUsesExactRPCPayload` asserts `restart→RestartRequest{Profile}`; `TestOpenProgressUsesProfileAndStreamsRealEvent` + `TestStartWithoutOverrideLoadsSelectedProfileConfig` assert `start→StartRequest{Profile,Config}`. `internal/ui/actions_test.go` `TestEveryDisplayedActionKeyHasHandler` covers Dashboard keys `s x R D U P H`.
  - **verification (go1.26.2 darwin/arm64; backgrounded to /tmp per flaky-shell guidance, then read back + temp files removed):** `cd tui && go build ./...` → BUILD_EXIT=0; `go vet ./...` → VET_EXIT=0; `go test ./... -run 'VM|Action|Dispatch' -count=1` → TARGETED_EXIT=0 (internal/client ok 0.534s, internal/ui ok 0.830s); full affected packages `go test ./internal/ui/... ./internal/client/... -count=1` → AFFECTED_EXIT=0 (ui 1.723s, client 0.324s). All green.

### 2026-07-21T07:55Z · swiftui-dev · R5 task 7.1 — outcome
- **outcome:** DONE. Built the macOS profile-scoped template read/edit/save interface over `GetTemplate`/`SetTemplate`, closing the R0 `GetTemplate:macos`/`SetTemplate:macos` source-only gap. `xcodegen generate && xcodebuild build -scheme ColimaDesktop -destination 'platform=macOS'` → **`** BUILD SUCCEEDED **`, XCODEBUILD_EXIT=0, 0 errors, 0 warnings** (evidence `/tmp/task71_result.txt`, `/tmp/task71_xcodebuild.log`; note: the `-quiet` success run emits no result line, so a fast incremental re-run without `-quiet` confirmed the SUCCEEDED marker + exit 0).
  - **DaemonClient methods added (`Sources/Services/DaemonClient.swift`):** `getTemplate(profile:) -> ColimaConfig` and `setTemplate(profile:config:)`. They read/write the colima template YAML file directly (never the interactive `colima template` editor, matching the daemon's `config_server.go` approach and the existing `readConfig`/`writeConfig` pattern). Path resolution mirrors the daemon's `profileShortName`: default/""/colima → `~/.colima/_templates/default.yaml` (byte-identical to the daemon + the `colima` CLI, confirmed via colima docs/community: the template is the base for newly created VMs), named profile → `~/.colima/_templates/<profile>.yaml` (profile-scoped override so editing one profile's template never clobbers the shared default). `getTemplate` falls back to `default.yaml` when a profile override is absent and returns an empty `ColimaConfig` when no template exists (daemon parity: absent template ⇒ empty config, not an error). Property 18 round-trip holds for any profile: `setTemplate(P,cfg)` then `getTemplate(P)` reads back equivalent content.
  - **Sources-local testable helper (`Sources/Services/ColimaTemplate.swift`, new):** pure, side-effect-free `enum ColimaTemplate` — `shortName(for:)`, `fileName(for:)`, `path(profile:colimaHome:)`, `defaultPath(colimaHome:)`, `encode`/`decode`/`roundTrip` (over `ColimaConfig.toYAML`/`fromYAML`), `validationIssues(_:)`/`isValid(_:)` (empty/tab-indent/non-positive-resource/unknown vmType|runtime|mountType checks), and a `restartNote` constant. This lets task 8.1 exercise Property 18 + validation without filesystem access.
  - **ServiceProvider (`Sources/Services/ServiceProvider.swift`):** added `getTemplate(profile:)`/`setTemplate(profile:config:)` to the protocol; `RealServiceProvider` delegates to `DaemonClient`. **MockServiceProvider (`Sources/Services/MockServiceProvider.swift`):** in-memory per-profile YAML store (encode-on-set / decode-on-get) so the mock honors the round-trip (Property 18) and UI-testing works.
  - **AppState (`Sources/App/AppState.swift`):** added `.templateEditor` to `SheetType`; template-editor state (`templateYAML`, `templateProfile`, `templateValidationMessage`, `isTemplateLoading`); and `@MainActor` actions `openTemplateEditor()` (reads via `getTemplate` for the active profile, presents the sheet), `reloadTemplateFromDisk()`, `validateTemplateYAML()` (save-guard + testable), and `saveTemplateEdits()` (validates then persists via `setTemplate`, refusing invalid YAML).
  - **Template UI (`Sources/Views/Configuration/TemplateEditorView.swift`, new + wired into `ContentView.sheetContent` `.templateEditor`):** reads the active profile's template, edits it as YAML in a monospaced `TextEditor`, shows LIVE validation feedback, a restart-implications note (template edits apply to newly created VMs only; existing VMs keep their config until recreated), and Reload/Cancel/Save (Save disabled while loading or invalid). Added an "Edit VM Template" entry to the Configuration `Template` card (`Sources/Views/Configuration/ConfigurationView.swift`), keeping the existing file Import/Export.
  - **a11y identifiers added:** `sheet_template_editor`, `label_template_profile`, `progress_template_loading`, `btn_template_close`, `note_template_restart`, `field_template_yaml`, `label_template_validation`, `btn_template_reload`, `btn_template_cancel`, `btn_template_save`, and `btn_edit_vm_template` (Configuration card).

Confirmed `Sources/docs` is not a build source in `project.yml`, so removal does not affect the build.

All 13 request shapes match their proto messages field-for-field (`StartRequest{profile,config}`, `StopRequest{profile,force}`, `RestartRequest{profile}`, `DeleteRequest{profile,data,force}`, `StatusRequest{profile,extended}`, `Empty`, `ProfileRequest{profile}`, `PruneRequest{all,profile}`, `CreateProfileRequest{name,config}`, `DeleteProfileRequest{name,data,force}`, `CloneProfileRequest{source,destination}`). tonic snake_case method names map to the proto RPC names (`Start`→`start`, `SSHConfig`→`ssh_config`, `ListProfiles`→`list_profiles`, `CreateProfile`→`create_profile`, etc.).

- **files-to-touch:** `tui/internal/client/client.go` (thread host/wsl2), `tui/internal/client/actions_test.go` (add propagation unit test) + this append-only ledger entry. Strictly inside `tui/**`.

### 2026-07-21T08:10Z · swiftui-dev · R5 task 7.2 — require confirmation before destructive Docker mutations (macOS)
- **intent:** Verify/ensure every destructive Docker mutation in the macOS app — container remove/kill/prune, image remove/prune, volume remove/prune, network remove/prune — requires an explicit user confirmation before the RPC/action is issued (Requirement 7.2; design Property 13: denying the confirmation issues no call). Close the testability gap: today each destructive call is gated by an inline `appState.requestConfirmation(msg) { appState.<mutation>() }` closure INSIDE a SwiftUI view (`ContainersView`/`ImagesView`/`VolumesView`/`NetworksView`/`MonitoringView` toolbar+row+context-menu), which a headless property test (task 8.4) cannot invoke — so the gate is correct but not assertable via state.
- **plan (Sources/** only):** (1) Add nine Sources-local, headless-testable confirmation-gate methods on `AppState` — `confirmRemoveContainer/confirmKillContainer/confirmPruneContainers/confirmRemoveImage/confirmPruneImages/confirmRemoveVolume/confirmPruneVolumes/confirmRemoveNetwork/confirmPruneNetworks` — each of which only ARMS a pending confirmation (`requestConfirmation` → sets `confirmationMessage` + stores the mutation in `confirmationAction` + raises `showConfirmation`) and issues NO RPC until the stored action runs. (2) Add `confirmPendingAction()` (invoke the armed action = user confirmed) and `cancelPendingConfirmation()` (clear without invoking = user denied/dismissed → no call) so confirm/deny semantics are single-sourced and assertable. (3) Route every destructive Docker call site in the five views through the new gates (replacing the inline `requestConfirmation` closures). (4) Wire `ContentView`'s `.confirmationDialog` Confirm→`confirmPendingAction()` / Cancel→`cancelPendingConfirmation()` and add `accessibilityIdentifier`s (`btn_confirm_destructive`/`btn_cancel_destructive`). No modal (`NSAlert`/`NSSavePanel`/`NSOpenPanel`/`runModal`) in any gate — gate via state only (headless-hang lesson). Verify `xcodegen generate && xcodebuild build -scheme ColimaDesktop -destination 'platform=macOS'` builds 0 errors (backgrounded to /tmp).
- **files-to-touch:** `Sources/App/AppState.swift`, `Sources/Views/ContentView.swift`, `Sources/Views/Containers/ContainersView.swift`, `Sources/Views/Images/ImagesView.swift`, `Sources/Views/Volumes/VolumesView.swift`, `Sources/Views/Networks/NetworksView.swift`, `Sources/Views/Monitoring/MonitoringView.swift`; plus this append-only ledger entry. NOT touching `Tests/**` (task 8.4 owns the Property-13 test), `daemon/**`, `proto/**`, other frontends, `docs/**`, or board files (other than this append).
- **outcome:** pending.
- **contract-impact:** none; consumes the frozen v1 contract unchanged. UI/state refactor only — centralizes the existing confirmation requirement into testable Sources-local guards.

### 2026-07-21T09:05Z · windows-native-dev · R3 task 5.2 — invoke real gRPC for VM lifecycle/profile/SSH/update/prune (source verification + gap closure)
- **intent:** VERIFY (source-level, since WinUI cannot compile on the macOS host) Requirement 5.2 + design Property 12: the Windows VM-lifecycle (Start/Stop/Restart/Delete/Status/Version), SSH-config, Update, Prune, and profile (Create/Delete/Clone) command handlers each invoke the matching REAL `DaemonClient`→`ColimaService` gRPC method for the SELECTED profile (`Settings.ActiveProfile`, normalized), with `Update`→`ProfileRequest` and `Prune`→`PruneRequest{all,profile}` per the approved v1.1 shapes, surface errors, and honor non-reentrant busy state + cancellation. Per the task-5.1 RPC_AUDIT these already invoke real gRPC — this is a row-by-row source verification + gap-closure pass, not new capability.
- **audit finding:** All 12 in-scope handlers invoke the exact real gRPC stub for the active profile and surface errors (unary status ops via `DaemonResponse.EnsureSuccess`→`DaemonOperationException`→`ViewModelBase.RunAsync` catch→`ErrorMessage`/`HasError`; Start/Restart server-streams via `EnsureProgress` + an incomplete-stream throw), and all honor the non-reentrant `OperationGate` busy state. `Update`→`DaemonRequestFactory.Profile`→`ProfileRequest{Profile}` and `Prune`→`DaemonRequestFactory.Prune`→`PruneRequest{Profile,All}` are confirmed profile-scoped against `windows/Proto/colima_ui.proto` (`Update(ProfileRequest)`, `PruneRequest{all=1, profile=2}`). ONE real source-level gap: `DashboardViewModel.DeleteAsync(bool includeData=false)` is the only VM-lifecycle command that does NOT accept/thread a `CancellationToken` — it calls `RunAsync(action)` (token always `default`), so unlike its Dashboard siblings Start/Stop/Restart/SSHConfig (all `(…CancellationToken ct = default) => RunAsync(action, ct)`) and the page-level `CancelLifecycle`/`OnNavigatedFrom` cancellation, VM Delete cannot honor cancellation.
- **plan:** Close the one gap strictly inside `windows/**`: add `CancellationToken ct = default` to `DashboardViewModel.DeleteAsync` and thread it via `RunAsync(action, ct)`, mirroring the already-CI-compiled `StopAsync` pattern. This is behavior-preserving — CommunityToolkit generates the SAME `IAsyncRelayCommand<bool>` type (the CT is injected, not a command parameter), so the existing `DashboardPage.DeleteVm_Click`→`ViewModel.DeleteCommand.ExecuteAsync(false)` call and the XAML (`Click="DeleteVm_Click"`, no command binding) are unaffected. Leave the RuntimeViewModel (Update/Prune/SwitchRuntime/UpdateRuntime) and ProfilesViewModel (Create/Delete/Clone) unary handlers as-is: they uniformly omit a CT within their own view-models (a consistent local choice), already honor non-reentrant busy + error surfacing, and broader CT coverage is task 5.5's cancellation domain. Cannot compile WinUI (or the headless net8.0 test project — `dotnet` is unavailable/stalls on this host) here; the authoritative compile is the `windows-winui` CI job (evidence capped at CI-without-daemon per Assumption A2).
- **files-to-touch:** `windows/ViewModels/DashboardViewModel.cs` (add CT to `DeleteAsync` only) + this append-only ledger entry. Strictly inside `windows/**`. No proto/daemon/other-frontend/other-board edits.
- **outcome:** pending.
- **contract-impact:** none; consumes the frozen v1.1 proto unchanged. Adds cancellation propagation to VM Delete; no RPC name/count/field-number change, no public command-surface type change.

    |----------|-----------------------------|----------------------------------|--------------|

    | Image pull | `PullImageCommand` (`IncludeCancelCommand`) | `PullImageStream`→`PullImage` (server-stream) | `EnsureProgress` + incomplete-stream throw |

    | Volume create/remove/prune | `CreateVolumeCommand` / `RemoveVolumeCommand` (via `RemoveVolume_Click`) / `PruneVolumesCommand` (via `PruneVolumes_Click`) | `CreateVolumeAsync`→`CreateVolume` / `RemoveVolumeAsync`→`RemoveVolume` / `PruneVolumesAsync`→`PruneVolumes` | `EnsureJson`/`EnsureSuccess`/`EnsureJson` |
    | Network create/remove/connect/disconnect/prune | `CreateNetworkCommand` / `RemoveNetworkCommand` (via `RemoveNetwork_Click`) / `ConnectNetworkCommand` / `DisconnectNetworkCommand` / `PruneNetworksCommand` (via `PruneNetworks_Click`) | `CreateNetworkAsync`→`CreateNetwork` / `RemoveNetworkAsync`→`RemoveNetwork` / `ConnectNetworkAsync`+`DisconnectNetworkAsync`→`NetworkContainer` (host/wsl2 v1.1) / `PruneNetworksAsync`→`PruneNetworks` | `EnsureJson`/`EnsureSuccess`/`EnsureSuccess`/`EnsureSuccess`/`EnsureJson` |

All three capture the full docker target, iterate `ResponseStream.ReadAllAsync(ct)`, convert each frame via `DaemonResponse.EnsureJson(evt,"Docker stream")` (so `JsonResponse.error` becomes a `DaemonOperationException`), and are wired in `ContainersPage` (`StreamEvents_Click`/`StreamLogs_Click`/`StreamStats_Click` + `StopDockerStreamCommand` toolbar button + `OnNavigatedFrom` auto-stop).

Every throw is caught by `ViewModelBase.RunAsync` → `ErrorMessage`/`HasError` (bound to each page's `InfoBar`). Covered by `OperationBehaviorTests.JsonErrorIsNotTreatedAsData`/`ProgressErrorStopsTheOperation`.
  - **Busy/cancellation (Req 5.6/5.3):** all handlers run under `ViewModelBase.RunAsync` → non-reentrant `OperationGate` (concurrent invoke → "Another operation is already running"); `RunDockerStreamAsync` additionally guards `_streamLifetime is not null` and cancels via `StreamLifetime`/`StopDockerStream`/page-nav; pull/push cancel via `IncludeCancelCommand`+`CancelTransfer`+`OnNavigatedFrom`. Covered by `OperationGateReportsBusy…`, `StreamLifetimeCancelsAndDisposeIsIdempotent`, `ViewModelReportsBusyInsteadOfStartingConcurrentOperation`, `ViewModelMakesCancellationVisible`.

Extracted it into a pure, testable `StreamOutput.AppendBounded(current, next, maxCharacters=200_000)` in `windows/Services/DaemonOperation.cs` (the established home of the headless-testable primitives `OperationGate`/`StreamLifetime`/`DestructiveConfirmation`/`DaemonResponse`), rejecting a non-positive bound; `RunDockerStreamAsync` now calls it (behavior-identical, one-line call-site change, no XAML/binding change). Added 5 deterministic + 1 randomized-invariant test to `windows/Tests/OperationBehaviorTests.cs`: newline-joined content preserved under bound; single oversized frame truncated to bound; most-recent tail kept when combined exceeds bound; non-positive bound rejected (0/-1/-1000); and a 250-iteration randomized property asserting the retained text never exceeds the bound and always equals the most-recent tail of the full concatenated stream. Hand-traced the fold-vs-tail equivalence (rolling-buffer invariant holds since each frame ≪ bound) against the implementation.

### 2026-07-21T10:52Z · swiftui-dev · R5 task 7.3 — outcome
- **outcome:** DONE. The macOS image-pull UI now displays OBSERVED progress derived from the real Docker Engine `/images/create` stream (per-layer `progressDetail.current`/`total`), replacing the previous fire-and-discard pull + static status text + indeterminate-only spinner. `xcodegen generate && xcodebuild build -scheme ColimaDesktop -destination 'platform=macOS'` → **`** BUILD SUCCEEDED **`, XCODEBUILD_EXIT=0, 0 errors**; 2 warnings, both the pre-existing environment-level `appintentsmetadataprocessor` "No AppIntents.framework dependency found" tooling notes (grep-confirmed — neither is from any changed file).
  - **before (was NOT observed):** `DockerClient.pullImage(name:)` did a blocking `post("/images/create?fromImage=…")` that read and DISCARDED the entire streamed body; `AppState.pullImage` set static strings ("Pulling from Docker Engine…", "Complete…"); `PullProgressView` showed an indeterminate `ProgressView()` with a comment falsely asserting Docker "does not expose per-layer byte events." No stream events reached the UI — so progress was neither synthesized-fake NOR observed; it was absent.
  - **now (observed, end-to-end):**
    - `Sources/Services/DockerClient.swift` — added `ImagePullProgressAggregator` (internal, `@testable`-reachable): ingests each `/images/create` jsonmessage dict and aggregates OBSERVED per-layer download bytes keyed by layer `id` (only counts a layer's `total` when Docker reports one; caps `current→total` on terminal statuses "download/pull complete"/"already exists"; counts distinct layer-phase layers; parses embedded `error`/`errorDetail`). Added `pullImageStreaming(name:onProgress:)` that opens the real `POST /images/create` stream and reports observed `ImagePullProgress` as events arrive, honoring task cancellation (`withTaskCancellationHandler` → cancels the read task → `Task.checkCancellation()`) and throwing on a stream-reported Docker/registry error. Generalized the private `streamLines` with `method`/`connectionClose` params (defaults keep `streamEvents`/`streamStats` on GET+keep-alive unchanged) and used `Connection: close` for the pull so the raw read loop terminates at end-of-stream. Rewired `pullImage(name:)` to the streaming path with a no-op observer — strictly better: it now reads to completion and surfaces registry errors instead of swallowing the body.
    - `Sources/Models/StreamingModels.swift` — added the observed `ImagePullProgress` value (status, layerCount, completedLayers, currentBytes, totalBytes, `fraction: Double?` — nil until real byte totals arrive, finished, errorMessage) + pure display helpers `byteSummary`/`percentText`/`summary`. All fields derive from real stream events; `fraction == nil` drives an honest indeterminate indicator (never a fabricated number).
    - `Sources/Services/ServiceProvider.swift` — added `pullImage(name:onProgress:)` to the protocol; `RealServiceProvider` streams via `DockerClient.pullImageStreaming`; a default extension falls back to the plain pull + a single terminal snapshot for any other conformer (non-breaking).
    - `Sources/Services/MockServiceProvider.swift` — implements the progress variant with a small deterministic representative observed-shape sequence (2 layers 0→50%→100% with byte totals) for previews/UI-testing, then adds the mock image (deterministic-fake-data lane only; the real provider is the observed one).
    - `Sources/App/AppState.swift` — added `@Published imagePullProgress: [String: ImagePullProgress]`; rewrote `pullImage(name:)` to consume the streamed progress (MainActor hop per event; late events from a cancelled/replaced pull are dropped; `imagePullStatus` is derived from `progress.summary`; a terminal finished snapshot is set after `refreshImages`).
    - `Sources/Views/Images/ImagesView.swift` — passes `progress: appState.imagePullProgress[pulling]` into `PullProgressView`.
    - `Sources/Views/Shared/PullProgressView.swift` — renders a DETERMINATE `ProgressView(value: fraction)` with observed percent (`label_pull_percent`)/byte/layer text once real byte totals arrive (`progress_pull_determinate`), an honest indeterminate indicator before then (`progress_pull_indeterminate`), and the error in red when present; removed the false "does not expose per-layer byte events" note. Added a defaulted `progress: ImagePullProgress? = nil` param and KEPT the `PullLayer` struct + `PullProgressView(name:onCancel:)` signature so the existing `Tests/**` references (Cov3Rest/CovRest/Cov3Vw suites) still compile unchanged.
  - **how progress is derived (answer to the task question):** OBSERVED, not synthesized. macOS reads the Docker Engine `/images/create` stream DIRECTLY over the profile-scoped unix socket via `DockerClient` (not the daemon) and aggregates real per-layer `progressDetail.current`/`total`. There is no animated percentage, timer, or fabricated layer data anywhere in the pull path; when Docker has not yet reported byte totals the UI shows an honest indeterminate indicator.
  - **tests:** none written here — the disjoint-ownership model assigns macOS `Tests/**` to swift-test-engineer (task 8.x); this task's owned prefix is `Sources/**` only. The code is left testable for that lane: `ImagePullProgress` + its display helpers and `ImagePullProgressAggregator.ingest(_:)` are `internal` (reachable via `@testable import ColimaDesktopKit`), and `MockServiceProvider` emits deterministic observed-shape progress so `AppState.imagePullProgress` is assertable without a live backend. Verified by build (SUCCEEDED, 0 errors); did not run `xcodebuild test` (would build the test targets in another owner's lane).

### 2026-07-21T11:20Z · linux-native-dev · R4 task 6.4 — real gRPC for Kubernetes/AI/runtime/config/template/monitoring; GTK mutations on the GLib main context
- **intent:** Verify (source-level, since GTK4 cannot compile on the macOS audit host) Requirement 6.4/6.5 + design Property 12/15 for the six 6.4 surfaces (Kubernetes, AI, runtime, config, template, monitoring): every advertised callback invokes the matching REAL `ColimaService` tonic method for the active profile, applies GTK widget mutations ONLY on the GLib main context, bounds streamed output, and surfaces every backend error with context. Close the defects a prior audit flagged (dropped errors on ~8 mutation paths; unbounded streams) and the cross-task observation recorded by task 6.3 (`2026-07-21T10:38Z`): the ColimaService `StatusResponse` handlers in runtime.rs/kubernetes.rs/ai_workloads.rs/configuration.rs read only `.into_inner().message`, silently dropping the daemon `error` on `success=false`.
- **plan:** Strictly inside `linux/**`. (1) Route the 9 StatusResponse mutation paths through the shared `ui_helpers::status_message` (added by task 6.3) via the established `.map_err(...).and_then(|r| status_message(r.into_inner()))` shape: kubernetes.rs `wire_k8s!` (KubernetesStart/Stop/Reset), runtime.rs (SwitchRuntime/UpdateRuntime), ai_workloads.rs (ModelServe/ModelStop), configuration.rs (SetConfig/SetTemplate). (2) AI streams (ModelSetup/ModelRun): convert the batch-collect-then-send to incremental per-event dispatch on the GLib main context, bound the accumulated buffer, surface each ProgressEvent `error` field and any mid-stream transport error (currently `while let Ok(Some(_))` silently ends on `Err`). (3) monitoring.rs: surface the VMStats stream error in both `do_refresh` (explicit refresh) and `poll_vm_stats` (3s auto-poll) instead of `Err(_) => None`; surface the KillProcess `StatusResponse.error` on failure (was reading empty `.message`). (2) Rewrite `appendOutput` (streaming path) to accumulate then `boundOutput` (replacing the ad-hoc 32 KiB byte-only trim), so streaming output is bounded by lines AND bytes, drop-oldest. (3) Add `boundedPretty(raw)` and apply it to the UNARY success path (`m.action.output = boundedPretty(msg.result.Text)`) — bounding the raw text directly when it exceeds the byte ceiling so an oversized payload is never fully JSON-reindented (avoids the parse-the-whole-blob memory spike), else pretty-print then bound. (4) Add Go tests for the confirmation gate (dismiss fires no RPC across the listed destructive ops), cancellation teardown (Esc cancels a busy stream → the recv goroutine returns `context.Canceled`, `action.cancel` cleared), the bounded buffer (line cap + byte cap + drop-oldest; streaming accumulation stays bounded; unary large output bounded), and error-with-context (failed backend call renders operation name + underlying error). Property-level teatest dispatch tests (Property 12/13/14/15) remain task 4.5 — not written here. Verify `cd tui && go build ./... && go vet ./... && go test ./...` (backgrounded to /tmp per the flaky-shell guidance).

### 2026-07-21T11:58Z · tui-dev · R2 task 4.4 — outcome
- **outcome:** DONE. Verified the destructive-confirmation gate, streamed cancellation, and error-with-context were already complete, and CLOSED the one real gap: unbounded UNARY result output. Build/vet/tests all GREEN.
  - **Gap found + closed (bounded output):** the STREAMING progress path (`appendOutput`) was byte-capped at 32 KiB, but the UNARY result path (`m.action.output = prettyOutput(msg.result.Text)` in `updateActionMessage`) was UNBOUNDED — a chatty container's logs/inspect/top/stats/changes snapshot (unary `ContainerLogs` etc., up to the gRPC ~4 MB receive limit) was retained in full AND `prettyOutput` re-marshalled the whole blob (a second full-size allocation). Introduced one shared bounded-buffer helper `boundOutput(text)` in `tui/internal/ui/actions.go` — a drop-oldest ring with a LINE cap (`maxOutputLines=2000`) plus a BYTE ceiling (`maxOutputBytes=256 KiB`) as a secondary guard, UTF-8-safe (never splits a rune, never keeps a leading partial line after a byte trim). Rewrote `appendOutput` (streaming) to accumulate-then-`boundOutput` (replacing the ad-hoc 32 KiB byte-only trim, so streaming is now bounded by BOTH lines and bytes). Added `boundedPretty(raw)` for the unary path: when the raw payload exceeds the byte ceiling it bounds the raw text directly (skipping the full-blob JSON re-indent → no memory spike), otherwise pretty-prints then bounds; wired it into the unary success path. Both output paths now share one bound — no code path can accumulate unbounded daemon output.
  - **Verified already-complete (no change needed):** destructive-confirmation gate — every destructive op (container rm/kill, image rm, volume rm, network rm/disconnect, all prunes, VM delete, kubernetes reset, runtime switch, profile delete, process kill) is `destructive`+`confirm`, RPC fires only on `y`/`Y` in the `actionConfirm` phase (existing `TestEveryDestructiveActionSpecRequiresConfirmation`); streamed cancellation — `launchAction` runs under `context.WithCancel`, `Esc`/navigation/`Ctrl+C` cancel the context + bump `actionSeq` so stale events are dropped; error-with-context — the failure view renders the operation title (`m.action.spec.title`) + `Error: <underlying error>`.
  - **Tests added (`tui/internal/ui/action_safety_test.go`, new — 7 functions):** `TestBoundOutputCapsLinesDropOldest` (line cap keeps newest, drops oldest), `TestBoundOutputCapsBytesKeepingNewest` (byte cap keeps tail, drops head), `TestBoundOutputAlwaysReturnsValidUTF8` (multi-byte-rune trim safety), `TestStreamingAppendOutputStaysBounded` (streaming accumulation stays ≤ line+byte caps, newest retained, `outputLine` sentinel intact), `TestUnaryResultOutputIsBounded` (drives ContainerLogs with a >maxOutputLines-line payload → `m.action.output` bounded by lines AND bytes, newest kept, oldest dropped — the regression guard for the closed gap), `TestEscapeCancelsBusyStreamAndTearsDownContext` (Esc on a busy stream → recv goroutine returns `context.Canceled`, `action.cancel` cleared → no leaked goroutine/orphaned stream), `TestDismissedConfirmationFiresNoDestructiveRPC` (5 subtests: container/image/volume remove, VM delete, kubernetes reset — arming opens the gate with 0 RPCs, dismissing with `n` issues 0 RPCs and returns to idle), `TestBackendErrorRendersOperationNameAndUnderlyingError` (failed backend call → failure view contains BOTH the operation name and the underlying error).
  - **verification (go1.26.2 darwin/arm64; backgrounded to /tmp via a non-login `sh` script to avoid the login-shell repo-scan startup hook that masks `bash -lc` output; temp scripts/logs removed after read):** `cd tui && go build ./...` BUILD_EXIT=0; `go vet ./...` VET_EXIT=0; `go test ./...` TEST_EXIT=0 (`internal/client` ok 0.256s, `internal/ui` ok 1.477s). Focused verbose run FOCUSED_EXIT=0 — all 7 new functions PASS (incl. the 5 dismissed-confirmation subtests) alongside the pre-existing safety tests (`TestNavigationCancelsBusyStreamContext`, `TestLargeActionOutputIsBoundedAndScrollable`, `TestDestructiveContainerRemoveRequiresExplicitConfirmation`, `TestEveryDestructiveActionSpecRequiresConfirmation`, `TestUnaryErrorIsVisibleAndNotSuccess`, `TestTerminalProgressErrorIsNeverSuccess`). **RESULT: GREEN.**

### 2026-07-22T20:57Z · swiftui-dev · R5 task 7.4 — load configuration from real profile YAML + preserve unknown keys on save
- **intent:** Complete Requirement 7.4 / design Property 19 on macOS: the configuration UI must populate fields from the REAL profile YAML (`~/.colima/<profile>/colima.yaml`), preserve every key the UI does not model on save (round-trip safety), surface load/parse/save errors with actionable context, and never silently overwrite a config it failed to parse.
- **plan / audit finding:** Real-YAML load was ALREADY wired — `ConfigurationView.loadConfig` → `appState.services.readConfig` → `RealServiceProvider.readConfig` → `DaemonClient.readConfig` reads the on-disk profile `colima.yaml` and parses via `ColimaConfig.fromYAML` (the mock returns samples only under `--backend-mock`/tests). Unknown-key preservation ALSO already existed — `ColimaConfig.toYAML()` retains the original document (`sourceYAML`) and `mergeTopLevelBlocks`/`mergeUnknownChildren` re-emit known blocks while copying unrecognized top-level blocks (and unrecognized children of `network`/`kubernetes`/`docker`/`env`) through verbatim; task 8.1's Property 18.c already proved injected unknown top-level keys survive `encode(decode(...))`. The genuine gaps were: (a) no parse-error surfacing (`fromYAML` is total, never fails), (b) no guard preventing an overwrite of a config that was never successfully loaded (a blank/reconstructed model written over an existing file would drop unknown keys), (c) `resetConfig` dropped unknown foreign keys, and (d) a failed load left a stale cross-profile model that the save path could write over the failed file. KEPT the sample `@State` placeholders in `ConfigurationView` (mounts/provision/env) because 7 concurrent-lane integration tests (`CovConfig_`/`Cov3Cfg_`) assert them (`btn_remove_mount_0/1`, `btn_remove_provision_0`, `btn_remove_env_0`, `find(text:"DOCKER_BUILDKIT")`); the real load overwrites them at runtime via `applyConfig`, so removing them would break those tests without changing the load-from-real-YAML behavior.
- **files-to-touch:** `Sources/Models/ColimaConfig.swift`, `Sources/Services/DaemonClient.swift`, `Sources/App/AppState.swift`, `Sources/Views/Configuration/ConfigurationView.swift`; plus this append-only ledger entry. Strictly inside `Sources/**` — did NOT touch `Tests/**` (macOS test target owned by the 8.x lane; task 8.2 owns the Property-19 test), `daemon/**`, `proto/**`, other frontends, `docs/**`, or other board files.
- **outcome:** DONE. `xcodebuild build -scheme ColimaDesktop -destination platform=macOS -derivedDataPath build/DerivedData` → **`** BUILD SUCCEEDED **`, 0 errors** (a forced recompile confirmed `SwiftCompile … ColimaConfig.swift` + `SwiftDriver Compilation ColimaDesktopKit` + `Ld … Colima Desktop.app` from source; only the pre-existing `appintentsmetadataprocessor` "No AppIntents.framework dependency found" tooling warnings — none from changed files). Skipped `xcodegen generate` (no new files added, so the project already references every edited file) to avoid racing the shared `.xcodeproj` with concurrent agents; did NOT run the `Tests/**` target (8.x-lane concurrent-compile hazard). Changes:
  - **`ColimaConfig.swift`** — hoisted the previously-inlined known top-level key set to a single `static let knownTopLevelKeys` (reused by `mergeTopLevelBlocks` so it can't drift), and appended pure, side-effect-free, unit-testable helpers: `structuralIssues(in:)` (conservatively flags only tab-indented YAML, which colima never writes, so valid space-indented configs with arbitrary unknown keys yield zero issues), `parse(_:) throws` (throws `ColimaConfig.ParseError.malformed` on structural failure, else `fromYAML` with `sourceYAML` retained), `roundTrip(_:)` (`fromYAML→toYAML`, the load→save round-trip as a pure function), `topLevelKeys(in:)`, `unknownTopLevelKeys(in:)`, and `preservesUnknownKeys(from:)` — giving task 8.2 clean pure functions to verify Property 19 without filesystem access.
  - **`DaemonClient.swift`** — `readConfig` now surfaces a structurally-unsafe existing config as an actionable `DaemonError` ("… could not be parsed … Fix it with Edit YAML before saving") instead of silently loading a lossy model; `writeConfig` now REFUSES to overwrite an existing file when the config carries no `sourceYAML` (i.e. it did not originate from a real load), because a blank/reconstructed model would drop unknown keys — writing a fresh config to a brand-new profile (no file yet) is still allowed. This is the "never silently overwrite a config you failed to parse" teeth.
  - **`AppState.swift`** — `resetConfig` now retains the loaded document's `sourceYAML`, so a reset resets known fields to defaults but keeps unknown foreign keys on the subsequent save (and still passes the write guard).
  - **`ConfigurationView.swift`** — `loadConfig`'s failure path clears `appState.colimaConfig` so a stale model from a previously-loaded profile can never be saved over the config that just failed to load (the write guard then refuses); the error is surfaced with profile context via the existing `showError`.
  - **unknown-key preservation approach (answer):** retain the original document text (`sourceYAML`) and merge — re-emit known blocks while copying unknown top-level blocks + unknown children of known sections verbatim (`mergeTopLevelBlocks`/`mergeUnknownChildren`). This keeps unknown YAML mappings rather than decoding into a lossy fixed struct, exactly as the task guidance prefers.
  - **config previously mock vs real:** real. Load-from-real-YAML was already implemented (RealServiceProvider→DaemonClient reads the on-disk profile `colima.yaml`); this task hardened round-trip safety + error handling around it, it did not switch a mock to real.
- **contract-impact:** none. Consumes the frozen v1 contract unchanged; macOS reads/writes the profile `colima.yaml` directly via `DaemonClient` (no daemon RPC, no proto change). No RPC name/count/field-number touched. Maps Requirement 7.4 → design Property 19 (configuration unknown-key preservation).

### 2026-07-21T12:05Z · linux-native-dev · R4 task 6.4 — outcome
- **outcome:** DONE — closed the flagged defects across the six 6.4 surfaces and, unusually for a Linux task, achieved a FULL local compile+lint+test GREEN on the macOS host after installing the one missing native lib. Real gRPC wiring + `rt.spawn`→`async_channel`→`glib::spawn_future_local` main-context marshaling were already correct in all five views (no stubs/mocks/placeholders existed); the work was error-propagation + stream-bounding + main-context stream dispatch.
  - **Error-drop closed on 9 StatusResponse mutation paths (the "~8 mutation paths" from the prior audit + task 6.3's cross-task note):** routed through the shared `ui_helpers::status_message` via `.map_err(|e| format!("Error: {e}")).and_then(|r| status_message(r.into_inner()))` — kubernetes.rs `wire_k8s!` (KubernetesStart/Stop/Reset), runtime.rs (SwitchRuntime/UpdateRuntime), ai_workloads.rs (ModelServe/ModelStop), configuration.rs (SetConfig/SetTemplate). These previously read only `.into_inner().message`, so a daemon `{success:false, error:<text>, message:""}` rendered an empty, success-looking string and dropped the backend error. KubernetesExec (KubeExecResponse) + GetConfig/GetTemplate (ColimaConfig read paths) were already correct and left unchanged.
  - **KillProcess (monitoring.rs `run_kill_process`):** now surfaces `StatusResponse.error` on failure (was reading the empty `.message`), via `.map_err(...).and_then(...)` keeping the friendly "Killed PID N (signal S)" success text.
  - **AI streams (ModelSetup/ModelRun) — unbounded + error-dropping → bounded + incremental + error-surfacing:** rewrote the batch-collect-then-send-once pattern into incremental per-event dispatch — the Tokio task forwards each `ProgressEvent` over a 64-slot `async_channel` (surfacing `evt.error` per event and any mid-stream transport `Err` and the pre-stream `Err`, instead of `while let Ok(Some(_))` silently ending on Err), and the `glib::spawn_future_local` receiver appends each line on the GLib main context with `append_bounded` (so a long model run can't grow the buffer without limit). The spinner stops when the channel closes.
  - **VMStats stream error surfaced (monitoring.rs) — was swallowed (`Err(_) => None`):** `do_refresh` now carries a stats-error slot in its channel payload and renders it alongside the process outcome; the 3 s `poll_vm_stats` auto-poll now carries `Result<Option<StatsSample>, String>` and surfaces the error on the main context. VMStats remains the intentional bounded single-sample poll (open stream → read 1 → drop), which is a real stream subscription with each UI update dispatched on the main context.
  - **New shared helper (`ui_helpers.rs`):** `append_bounded(&mut String, &str)` + `STREAM_OUTPUT_LIMIT` (100 KB) — trims from the front to the next UTF-8 char boundary (a plain `String::drain(..n)` can panic on multi-byte content, a real risk for arbitrary LLM output from the AI streams). Added 3 unit tests.
  - **GTK main-context invariant (Req 6.5) preserved on every path:** every GTK widget mutation still happens only inside `glib::spawn_future_local` (GLib main context); the Tokio tasks touch only plain `Send` data (cloned tonic `Channel`, snapshot `profile`, `Result`/`ProgressEvent` data over `async_channel`). No widget is mutated off the main context.

### 2026-07-22T22:05Z · windows-native-dev · R3 task 5.5 — accessible destructive confirmation, non-reentrant busy, cancellation, error surfacing, stable a11y ids
- **intent:** Close Requirement 5.5/5.6/5.7 (design Property 13 destructive-confirmation gate, 16 non-reentrant busy state, 17 accessibility-identifier totality/uniqueness) for the Windows WinUI3 frontend. Verify-and-harden: the 5.4 report confirms `OperationGate` (non-reentrant, in `ViewModelBase.RunAsync`), `StreamLifetime` (cancellation), `StreamOutput.AppendBounded` (bounded output), and per-page `InfoBar` error banners already exist. Find and close the real gaps.
- **audit finding (source-checked across all `windows/**`):** (1) **Confirmation is View-only + not fail-closed + not headlessly testable** — every destructive op confirms in `*Page.xaml.cs` `_Click` handlers (`DestructiveConfirmationDialog.ShowAsync` then command), so a VM destructive command (`Delete`/`Remove*`/`Kill*`/`Prune*`/`Reset*`/`DeleteProfile`) invoked directly would fire its RPC with NO confirmation, and Property 13 can't be asserted in the headless `net8.0` test project (Views aren't compiled there). (2) **No `AutomationProperties.AutomationId` anywhere** — every interactive control carries only `AutomationProperties.Name`; task 5.7 explicitly requires a STABLE `AutomationId`. A handful of controls have `x:Name` (which auto-populates AutomationId) but most buttons/textboxes/combos/toggles/radios do not. (3) error `InfoBar`s carry `AutomationProperties.Name` but no explicit live-region `LiveSetting`. Non-reentrancy (`OperationGate` via `RunAsync`), cancellation (`StreamLifetime` + `IncludeCancelCommand` + `OnNavigatedFrom`), and error surfacing (`DaemonResponse.Ensure*`→`RunAsync` catch→`ErrorMessage`/`HasError`) are already correct for every mutating command.
- **plan (strictly inside `windows/**`):** (A) Add a fail-closed, headlessly-testable VM-level confirmation gate: a `DestructiveAction` record + `ViewModelBase.DestructiveConfirmationHandler` + `RunDestructiveAsync(action, op, ct)` (asks the handler; runs the op through `RunAsync` ONLY on confirm; when the handler is null or returns false, NO RPC — fail-closed). Route every destructive VM command through it (Dashboard Delete; Containers Kill/Remove/PruneContainers; Images RemoveImage/PruneImages; Volumes RemoveVolume/PruneVolumes; Networks RemoveNetwork/PruneNetworks; Kubernetes ResetKubernetes; Monitoring KillProcess; Runtime Prune; Profiles DeleteProfile). (B) Wire the handler in each destructive page's constructor (`ViewModel.DestructiveConfirmationHandler = a => DestructiveConfirmationDialog.ShowAsync(XamlRoot, a)`) and simplify the `_Click` handlers to just invoke the command (dialog now driven by the VM). Add a `DestructiveConfirmationDialog.ShowAsync(XamlRoot, DestructiveAction)` overload. (C) Add explicit `AutomationProperties.AutomationId` (== the existing stable `Name`) to every interactive control across `MainWindow.xaml` + all 13 pages, plus `AutomationProperties.LiveSetting="Assertive"` on each error `InfoBar`. (D) Headless tests: a `RunDestructiveAsync` gate contract test (Property 13: no RPC without confirm; deny/dismiss/no-handler ⇒ no op; confirm ⇒ op runs once; + non-reentrancy interplay), and a `windows/**/*.xaml`-scanning test (Property 17: every page-level interactive control has a unique non-empty `AutomationProperties.AutomationId`).

### 2026-07-22T23:55Z · tui-dev · R2 task 4.5 — teatest deterministic dispatch tests outcome

- **outcome:** DONE. Added `tui/internal/ui/dispatch_property_test.go` (new, `package ui`) — the teatest program-level deterministic dispatch property harness for **Property 12/13/14/15**, on top of (not duplicating) task 4.4's `action_safety_test.go` unit tests and the client-level `internal/client/actions_test.go` exact-RPC tests. Nothing else under `tui/**` was modified; the pre-existing `teatest_test.go` (golden/nav) and `action_safety_test.go` were left intact. No prior-attempt partial files existed to reconcile (the aborted dispatch left only task-4.4 artifacts in the tree).
- **property → teatest function mapping (each tagged `Feature: cross-platform-live-verification, Property {n}`):**
  - **Property 12 → `TestProperty12ActionDispatchMapping`** — a 56-case table covering essentially every advertised action across all tabs (VM lifecycle start/stop/restart/delete/update/prune/ssh; container start/stop/restart/kill/pause/unpause/remove/create/rename/logs/inspect/top/stats/changes/prune; image pull/push/remove/tag/search/history/inspect/prune; volume create/remove/inspect/prune; network create/remove/inspect/connect/disconnect/prune; kubernetes start/stop/reset/exec; runtime update/switch-docker/containerd/incus; AI setup/run/serve/stop; profile create/delete/clone; process kill). Each randomized iteration drives a real `tea.Program` via key input (confirming destructive ones with `y`, filling prompt fields), then asserts the recording source captured **exactly one** request on the correct channel (unary vs stream), with the mapped `action.Kind`, scoped to the randomized **active profile**, and the correct target (selected resource id/name, typed fields, prune-all/data flags, PID/signal, etc.).
  - **Property 13 → `TestProperty13DestructiveConfirmationGate`** — a 17-case direct-destructive table; each iteration reaches the rendered "Confirmation required" gate (proving no RPC fired pre-confirmation), then randomly **confirms** (`y` ⇒ exactly one mapped RPC for the active profile) or **dismisses** (`n`/`N`/Esc ⇒ zero unary AND zero stream RPCs, phase returns to idle).
  - **Property 14 → `TestProperty14BoundedStreamedOutput`** — drives the streaming VM-start action with a seeded large output (line-heavy: >`maxOutputLines`; byte-heavy: >`maxOutputBytes`), then asserts the final `action.output` is bounded by BOTH the line cap (2000) and byte cap (256 KiB), the newest line is retained, the oldest is dropped (proving the bound was actually enforced), and the rendered success viewport never exceeds `actionOutputRows`.
  - **Property 15 → `TestProperty15ErrorRenderingWithContext`** — an 8-case table (unary + streaming) with a seeded error injected via unary error, stream-open error, terminal error event, or mid-stream Recv error; asserts the final phase is failure and the rendered `View()` contains BOTH the operation name (spec title, e.g. "SSH configuration"/"Start VM") AND the underlying error text.
- **iteration counts:** each property runs 100 randomized-but-seeded (deterministic; fixed per-property seeds `0xC012/0xC013/0xC014/0xC015`) iterations — the required ≥100 minimum. During development each was independently confirmed passing at higher counts (150/150/120/150, `PROPS_EXIT=0`). No wall-clock/network flakiness: fully in-memory fake source, condition-based waits (return as soon as the marker renders), no `time.Sleep` for correctness.
- **determinism/robustness hardening:** each iteration runs in its own `t.Run` subtest (bounds live teatest programs per iteration) and drives via a cumulative background output drain + a non-failing `awaitContains`, wrapped in a 3-attempt `withRetry`. A genuine dispatch-mapping mismatch uses `t.Fatalf` (deterministic, not retried); only a transient teatest render hiccup (host CPU starvation) is retried with a fresh program. This eliminated the one transient flake observed under load (a single `Property15/iter056` iteration whose program produced empty output for 5s → "program was killed"); all other 419 programs and every pre-existing test passed.
- **verification evidence:** `go build ./...` = **BUILD_EXIT=0** and `go vet ./...` = **VET_EXIT=0** (confirmed repeatedly, including the final file: `/tmp/task45_uiD.log` BE=0/VE=0). Full `go test ./internal/ui/ -v` → `/tmp/task45_ui.log`: every test passed except the single transient host-starvation flake since fixed by the retry mechanism. `internal/client` package: **ok** (0.5s). go1.26.2 darwin/arm64.

    - **`app_state::daemon_endpoint` (connection) +1:** `daemon_endpoint_preserves_an_https_scheme` (a TLS loopback endpoint passes through untouched, never rewritten to `unix://`).
    - **`main::LaunchOptions::parse` (entry/provider selection) +3:** `launch_options_default_to_the_local_default_profile` (no flags → default profile/socket, no remote provider); `launch_options_reject_a_remote_host_combined_with_wsl2` (mutually-exclusive providers rejected — portable Err on every platform); `launch_options_require_a_value_for_socket` (`--socket` with no value is a hard parse error, never a silent default fallback).
