### 2026-07-23T02:05Z · windows-native-dev · R3 task 5.6 — Windows view-model/service tests (native `windows-winui` CI runner)

- **intent:** Round out the automated test suite for the Windows WinUI3 view-models and services so the native `windows-winui` CI runner (and the headless `net8.0` `windows/Tests` project, which also runs on this macOS host via `dotnet`) has MEANINGFUL per-view-model coverage: view-model command behavior, request/scope construction, response-error handling, streaming/cancellation, bounded output, and the destructive-confirmation gate. The headless `windows/Tests` project has 42 passing tests (tasks 5.1–5.5) that exercise the SHARED seams (`ViewModelBase.RunDestructiveAsync`, `DaemonRequestFactory`, `DaemonResponse`, `OperationGate`, `StreamLifetime`, `StreamOutput`, XAML AutomationId inventory). The gap: no per-view-model command tests for Kubernetes/AI/Runtime/Config/Monitoring, Profiles, Machines (and Dashboard/Containers/Images/Volumes/Networks) that assert each `[RelayCommand]` invokes exactly its mapped, profile/target-scoped RPC (Property 12), gates destructive commands (Property 13), is non-reentrant (Property 16), surfaces backend errors, and bounds/cancels streams. Requirement 5.8; design Property 12/13/16/17.
- **root cause the tests need:** view-models call `App.DaemonClient` (a sealed concrete `DaemonClient`, real `GrpcChannel`) through `ViewModelBase.Client`, with NO seam to inject a recording/fake client — so per-command dispatch cannot be asserted headlessly today. Introduce a minimal dependency-inversion seam (`IDaemonClient`) so a recording fake can stand in, WITHOUT making the headless project depend on the Windows SDK.
- **plan (strictly inside `windows/**`):**
  1. **Production seam (behavior-preserving):** add `windows/Services/IDaemonClient.cs` declaring the full public consumer surface of `DaemonClient` (all ~50 Colima/Docker helper + stream methods used by the view-models, plus `CurrentAddress`/`Reconnect`/`CheckHealthAsync`, `: IDisposable`); make `DaemonClient : IDaemonClient` (it already implements every member — signatures mirrored exactly). Change `ViewModelBase.Client` return type `DaemonClient` → `IDaemonClient` (real app: `App.DaemonClient` is concrete `DaemonClient`, upcasts fine; test stub: typed `IDaemonClient`). Change `DaemonConnectionManager.ConnectAsync`'s first param `DaemonClient` → `IDaemonClient` (callers pass a concrete `DaemonClient`/fake — both are `IDaemonClient`). Change the headless test stub `windows/Tests/ProductionAppStub.cs` `App.DaemonClient` to `IDaemonClient`. **Real `App.xaml.cs` is NOT touched** (keeps `public static DaemonClient DaemonClient`; the concrete type implements the interface). Because the headless `windows/Tests` project compiles the SAME production `Services/*.cs` + `ViewModels/*.cs`, a GREEN headless build transitively proves `IDaemonClient.cs`/`DaemonClient.cs`/`DaemonConnectionManager.cs`/`ViewModelBase.cs` + all view-models compile; only XAML/`App.xaml.cs`/Views (untouched) remain WinUI-only.
  2. **Tests:** add a recording `windows/Tests/Fakes/RecordingDaemonClient.cs` (records each call's method + profile/host/wsl2 + args; returns canned success responses; configurable to throw `DaemonOperationException` for error-path tests; server-streaming methods return an `AsyncServerStreamingCall<T>` backed by a scripted/blocking `IAsyncStreamReader<T>` so streaming + cancellation are deterministic). Then per-view-model test files asserting: **Property 12** each command → exactly the mapped `ColimaService`/`DockerService` RPC, scoped to the active profile/docker target (Kubernetes, AI, Runtime, Config, Monitoring, Profiles, Machines, Dashboard, Containers, Images, Volumes, Networks); **Property 13** each destructive command gated (deny/no-handler ⇒ no RPC; confirm ⇒ one RPC); **Property 16** non-reentrant busy; response-error handling (thrown `DaemonOperationException` ⇒ `HasError`/`ErrorMessage`); streaming completion/cancellation + bounded output. Keep the existing 42 tests intact.
  3. If any test genuinely needs the WinUI runtime/UIA (native-only), author it clearly separated + documented as `windows-winui`-CI-only; do NOT make the headless project depend on the Windows SDK.
- **files-to-touch:** NEW `windows/Services/IDaemonClient.cs`; EDIT `windows/Services/DaemonClient.cs` (`: IDaemonClient`), `windows/Services/DaemonConnectionManager.cs` (param type), `windows/ViewModels/ViewModelBase.cs` (`Client` return type), `windows/Tests/ProductionAppStub.cs` (stub field type); NEW `windows/Tests/Fakes/RecordingDaemonClient.cs` + new per-view-model test files under `windows/Tests/`. ### 2026-07-23T01:10Z · tui-dev · R2 task 4.6 — gated live PTY lane against desktop-e2e

- **intent:** Add the release-gating **live** end-to-end lane that drives the *compiled* TUI binary through a real pseudo-terminal (PTY) against a real colima-desktop daemon bound to the disposable `desktop-e2e` colima profile — actual key input -> real gRPC -> real rendered output — as the `live-backend` evidence complement to the deterministic teatest dispatch tests (task 4.5, fake source). Requirement 4.7; design R4 mapping "teatest dispatch tests + live PTY lane on `desktop-e2e` | deterministic -> live-backend". The lane MUST be double-gated and SKIP cleanly (never fail, never hang) when the live env is absent, so the default `go test ./...` stays green on this host where `desktop-e2e` is not yet provisioned (task 10.1 provisions it; task 10.x drives this lane live).
- **plan:** Strictly inside `tui/**` (+ `tui/go.mod`/`go.sum` for the PTY dep, + this ledger append). (1) New package `tui/internal/e2e` with an always-compiled `doc.go` (so the dir is a valid, buildable package with or without the tag) + a `//go:build live_e2e` `live_pty_test.go` holding the single `TestLivePTYEndToEnd`. (2) Add the PTY lib — `go.mod` had no `creack/pty`, so add `github.com/creack/pty` pinned to an exact version + `go mod tidy`. (3) Double gate: (a) build tag `live_e2e` (file not compiled into the default test binary at all), (b) runtime `t.Skip` unless `COLIMA_DESKTOP_E2E=1` AND `~/.colima/<profile>/docker.sock` (profile default `desktop-e2e`, override `COLIMA_DESKTOP_E2E_PROFILE`) exists and is a socket. (4) Self-contained daemon wiring: if `COLIMA_DESKTOP_ENDPOINT` is set connect to that already-running daemon (task 10.x owns the lifecycle), else build+start the daemon on a private short-path `/tmp` Unix socket (macOS ~104-byte sun_path limit) and gate on socket readiness. (5) Drive via `pty.StartWithSize` (120x40): launch -> assert dashboard + real `profile=<p>` status line (live Status RPC) -> key `2` navigate to Containers + assert real list render (ListContainers) -> key `1`+`H` run one NON-destructive action (SSH configuration) end-to-end to `Success:` -> key `q` clean teardown asserting prompt process exit. Verify build/vet/vet-`-tags live_e2e`/default-test-skip via a backgrounded non-login `sh` to /tmp logs (execute_bash here is masked by a zsh login hook).
- **files-to-touch:** `tui/internal/e2e/doc.go` (new), `tui/internal/e2e/live_pty_test.go` (new, `//go:build live_e2e`), `tui/go.mod` + `tui/go.sum` (add `creack/pty`), plus this append-only ledger entry. NOT touching daemon/, windows/, linux/, Sources/, Tests/, proto/, or other board files (building the sibling daemon binary from the test compiles its source into a temp binary; it never writes into `daemon/`).
- **outcome:** DONE — RESULT: **GREEN**. Added the gated live PTY lane; the default build/vet/test stay green with the lane skipped cleanly.
  - **Dependency:** added `github.com/creack/pty v1.1.24` (exact pin) as a DIRECT require in `tui/go.mod` (kept by `go mod tidy`, which considers all build tags), with both hashes in `tui/go.sum` (`h1:bJrF4RRfyJnbTJqzRLHzcGaZK1NeM5kTC9jGgovnR1s=` + `/go.mod`).
  - **Gating mechanism (build tag + env + socket):** the harness lives behind `//go:build live_e2e`, so the default `go build ./...` / `go vet ./...` / `go test ./...` never compile it (the `internal/e2e` package is just `doc.go` -> `[no test files]`). Even with `-tags live_e2e`, `TestLivePTYEndToEnd` runtime-skips unless `COLIMA_DESKTOP_E2E=1` AND `~/.colima/desktop-e2e/docker.sock` is a live socket. Proven: `go test -tags live_e2e -run TestLivePTYEndToEnd -v ./internal/e2e/` -> `--- SKIP: TestLivePTYEndToEnd (0.00s) ... PASS ok 0.485s` (never fails, never hangs) on this host where `desktop-e2e` is absent.
  - **How it is driven live in task 10.x:** with `desktop-e2e` up (task 10.1 `scripts/live/e2e-env.sh`), run `COLIMA_DESKTOP_E2E=1 go test -tags live_e2e ./tui/internal/e2e/`.     - **`ViewModelDestructiveDispatchTests.cs` (+4, Property 13 confirmation gate per command):** a 15-command table (Dashboard.Delete, Kubernetes.Reset, Runtime.Prune/PruneAll, Monitoring.Kill, Profiles.Delete, Containers.Kill/Remove/Prune, Images.Remove/Prune, Volumes.Remove/Prune, Networks.Remove/Prune) proves each concrete destructive command routes through `RunDestructiveAsync`: denied ⇒ ZERO daemon calls (fail-closed, incl. no follow-up refresh); no-handler ⇒ ZERO calls; confirmed ⇒ exactly the mapped RPC. Seeded `P17RNG` (SplitMix64) makes iterations reproducible. Tests, each tagged `Feature: cross-platform-live-verification, Property 17`:

  1. `scripts/live/e2e-env.sh` with idempotent subcommands: `up` (stop OrbStack if running + RECORD restore state; ensure `colima`+`docker` installed via Homebrew, install if missing; start ONLY the `desktop-e2e` profile — modest 2 CPU / 2 GiB / 20 GiB, `--vm-type vz`, `--mount-type virtiofs`, `--runtime docker` on FRESH creation, plain restart if it already exists so an existing profile is never destroyed/reshaped; block on the profile-scoped socket `~/.colima/desktop-e2e/docker.sock`, NEVER the ambient `/var/run/docker.sock`); `status` (running/stopped/absent + prints the docker socket path + whether it responds); `down` (stop only — reversible); `teardown` (best-effort remove `e2e-` resources + `colima delete --profile desktop-e2e --force` to leave the host clean); `restore-orbstack` (restart OrbStack from the recorded state); `guard <profile>` (exit 0 iff `profile == desktop-e2e`, else reject — the hard invariant baked in here; task 10.2 adds the property test); `evidence` (write `colima status --json` + `docker info`/`version` to the gitignored live evidence area).
  2. HARD SAFETY INVARIANTS: `E2E_PROFILE` is a readonly literal; EVERY mutating colima call goes through a `colima_e2e` wrapper that calls `guard` and injects `--profile desktop-e2e` — so no other profile (`default`/`cdtest`/`staging`) can ever be created/stopped/deleted. All docker calls pin `DOCKER_HOST=unix://<profile-sock>` (this host's active docker context is `default *`→`/var/run/docker.sock` with no daemon since OrbStack is stopped, so ANY unpinned `docker` call HANGS — pinning is both correctness and safety). No `timeout` binary on host → a `run_to` helper bounds each docker call. Idempotent: `up` when up = no-op; `down`/`teardown` when already down/absent = success. Reversibility: OrbStack stop recorded to a state file + `restore-orbstack`.
  3. sudo: prefer non-sudo (colima runs rootless in the user context; `desktop-e2e` is already running without sudo on this host). The AGENTS.md sudo password is used non-interactively only if genuinely required, and NEVER echoed to logs/ledger/evidence.
  4. Evidence hygiene (R8.6): the script self-ignores its evidence dir (`artifacts/live/.gitignore` = `*`) so host-specific paths/data (`/Users/...`, sockets) never get committed; `.gitignore` at repo root already ignores nothing under `artifacts/`, so the self-ignore is the guard.
  5. Makefile: add `live-e2e-up`/`live-e2e-status`/`live-e2e-down`/`live-e2e-teardown` targets.
  6. `scripts/verify.sh` is ALREADY socket-gated (auto-detects `~/.colima/desktop-e2e/docker.sock` and runs the RealBackend e2e lane) — confirmed by read; NOT modifying it (already correct + it is the green gate; editing risks a regression and is outside `scripts/live/**`).
- **verify by ACTUALLY running it (live task):** run `scripts/live/e2e-env.sh up` (backgrounded to `/tmp/task101_up.log`), poll patiently (colima start can take minutes), confirm the `desktop-e2e` profile is up and its docker socket responds (`colima status --json`, `docker info` via the socket), and capture the real evidence. Also verify idempotency (`up` twice = no-op) and `status`.
- **files-to-touch:** `scripts/live/e2e-env.sh` (new), `Makefile` (add live-e2e targets) + this append-only ledger entry. NOT touching the frontend source trees (`daemon/`, `tui/`, `windows/`, `linux/`, `Sources/`, `Tests/`), `proto/`, `scripts/verify.sh`, or other board files.
- **host preflight (read-only, before acting):** `colima 0.10.1` + `docker`/`limactl`/`qemu-img`/`brew` installed; profiles `default`(Stopped)/`cdtest`(Stopped)/`desktop-e2e`(**Running**, 4CPU/8GiB/20GiB, docker)/`staging`(Stopped); `desktop-e2e` docker.sock present and `docker info` via it returns `Name=colima-desktop-e2e` server 29.2.1; OrbStack process NOT running (`orbctl` at ~/bin/orbctl, orbstack docker context exists). ### 2026-07-23T12:20Z · devops · R6 task 10.2 — implement the profile safety guard (hard invariant) outcome

- **outcome:** DONE — RESULT: **GREEN**. The desktop-e2e-only invariant is now a HARD, centralized, enforced choke-point that every live-mutating entry point routes through; verified live that the guard accepts `desktop-e2e` and rejects all other targets, and the running `desktop-e2e` profile was left UP and responding (not disrupted).
  - **How the invariant is enforced (constant + choke-point):**
    - **Single source of truth:** the allowed profile is defined ONCE, as a locked readonly literal `E2E_PROFILE="desktop-e2e"` in the new `scripts/live/guard.sh`. It CANNOT be overridden by env or args: the loader is fail-closed — if the environment pre-seeds `E2E_PROFILE` to anything other than `desktop-e2e`, sourcing/executing refuses to run (`return 1 2>/dev/null || exit 1`); an inherited value equal to `desktop-e2e` is accepted; a re-source is idempotent (`_LIVE_GUARD_LOADED` guard). `e2e-env.sh` and the `Makefile` no longer carry their own literal — `e2e-env.sh` sources `guard.sh`, and the Makefile derives `E2E_PROFILE := $(shell bash scripts/live/guard.sh profile 2>/dev/null || echo desktop-e2e)`.
    - **The guard:** `guard <profile>` returns 0 iff `<profile>` is EXACTLY `desktop-e2e`, else prints `[live-guard] SAFETY: refusing live action against profile '<x>' (only 'desktop-e2e' is allowed)` to stderr and returns 1. Pure + read-only (string compare only; no colima/docker call). Exact-match, so `default`, empty, `Default`, `DESKTOP-E2E`, `desktop-e2e ` (trailing space), `../desktop-e2e`, `desktop-*`, etc. are all rejected.
    - **The choke-point (why nothing can escape it):** the ONLY sanctioned mutators live in `guard.sh` — `colima_e2e` asserts the guard, hard-injects `--profile desktop-e2e`, AND rejects any caller-supplied `--profile`/`-p` (so a trailing `--profile default` can't override the injected target); `docker_e2e` asserts the guard, pins `DOCKER_HOST=unix://~/.colima/desktop-e2e/docker.sock`, AND rejects any caller `-H`/`--host`/`-c`/`--context` (so docker can't be redirected to another daemon). `e2e-env.sh` now sources `guard.sh`, dropped its duplicate `guard`/`colima_e2e`/`E2E_PROFILE`, and routes its docker calls (verify_socket/status/evidence/cleanup) through `docker_e2e`. For the R9 harness (task 10.6, which lives outside `scripts/live/`), `guard.sh` provides a launcher choke-point: `guard.sh exec -- <cmd...>` (also `e2e-env.sh harness-exec -- <cmd...>`) guards, exports the locked safe env (`E2E_PROFILE`, `DOCKER_HOST`=profile socket, `TEST_RUNNER_COLIMA_DESKTOP_TEST_PROFILE=desktop-e2e`, `TEST_RUNNER_COLIMA_DESKTOP_REAL_E2E=1`), then execs — there is no profile argument, so a harness invoked through it can only ever run `desktop-e2e`.
  - **Guard accept/reject evidence (live, backgrounded to /tmp, non-login `bash --noprofile --norc`):**
    - `scripts/live/e2e-env.sh guard desktop-e2e` → **rc 0** (accept).
    - `scripts/live/e2e-env.sh guard default` → **rc 1**, stderr `…SAFETY: refusing live action against profile 'default' (only 'desktop-e2e' is allowed)`.
    - `scripts/live/e2e-env.sh guard ""` (empty) → **rc 1** + SAFETY message.
    - `scripts/live/e2e-env.sh guard prod` → **rc 1** + SAFETY message.
    - `scripts/live/guard.sh guard desktop-e2e` → **rc 0**; `scripts/live/guard.sh guard default` → **rc 1** + SAFETY. (Same invariant via both the lifecycle entry point and the shared library directly.)
    - `scripts/live/guard.sh selftest` → **29/29 checks pass, rc 0** (`passed=29 failed=0`): guard accepts `desktop-e2e`; rejects 18 non-`desktop-e2e` variants (default/empty/whitespace/case/prefix/suffix/path-traversal/glob); rejection prints SAFETY; constant locked + socket profile-scoped; `colima_e2e` injects `--profile desktop-e2e` and rejects caller `--profile`/`-p` WITHOUT calling colima (hermetic PATH-shadowed fake); `docker_e2e` pins DOCKER_HOST and rejects `-H`/`--context` WITHOUT calling docker; and subprocess env-lock proof (`env E2E_PROFILE=default bash guard.sh profile` refuses fail-closed, `env E2E_PROFILE=desktop-e2e …` accepted). `make live-e2e-selftest` runs the same battery.
  - **Live profile left UP (not disrupted) — confirmed AFTER the work:** `scripts/live/e2e-env.sh status` → `profile: desktop-e2e / status: running / docker socket: /Users/<user>/.colima/desktop-e2e/docker.sock / socket present: yes / docker responds: yes`, rc 0. The refactored `docker_e2e` path was exercised against the REAL socket by this `status` run (docker info returned via the pinned socket), proving the routing works without touching any other profile. `guard`/`selftest`/`status` are all read-only; `up`/`down`/`teardown` were NOT run, so the running VM was untouched.
  - **verification hygiene:** `bash -n` clean on both scripts; `make -n test-real-e2e` confirms the Makefile now derives `TEST_RUNNER_COLIMA_DESKTOP_TEST_PROFILE=desktop-e2e` from `guard.sh profile`; no bare `COLIMA_HOME` reference remains after the refactor; shellcheck not installed on host (skipped — `bash -n` + the live selftest cover parsing + behavior). The full randomized Property 1 + Property 2 tests (≥100 iterations) are task 10.3's deliverable (not written here); this task ships the fixed accept/reject self-check the guard needs as a hard invariant.
  - **console-noise note:** the host shell prints an EXIT-trap line ("cleanup done" / job-control notice) after each `execute_bash`; all real output/return codes were captured to /tmp log files and read back (the files show the true results above).
- **files touched:** `scripts/live/guard.sh` (new, +x — locked constant + guard + colima_e2e/docker_e2e + exec/env/selftest CLI), `scripts/live/e2e-env.sh` (source guard.sh; dropped duplicate constant/guard/colima_e2e; docker calls routed via docker_e2e; +`selftest`/+`harness-exec` subcommands + usage/header updates), `Makefile` (E2E_PROFILE derived from guard.sh single source of truth; +`live-e2e-selftest` target) + these two append-only ledger entries. NOT touched: `scripts/verify.sh` (out of lane; its profile ref is a read-only socket-existence check and its RealBackend lane is already pinned to `desktop-e2e`), `scripts/ci/**`, `scripts/tests/**`, the frontend trees (`daemon/`, `tui/`, `windows/`, `linux/`, `Sources/`, `Tests/`), `proto/`, other board files.
- **contract-impact:** none; ops/safety tooling only. Consumes the frozen v1.1 contract + the `~/.colima/desktop-e2e/docker.sock` convention unchanged (no RPC name/count/field-number touched). The disposable `desktop-e2e` profile is the ONLY colima state any live-mutating entry point can target — enforced by one constant in one choke-point. INJECT FAKES ONLY — a file-private `FakeProbe: DependencyProbe` (drives `probe(_:)`/`isPackageManagerAvailable()`/`isOnline()` from fixed values) + a lock-protected `RecordingInstaller: DependencyInstaller` (records `run` call-count + path, configurable to succeed / throw a specific `DependencyInstallError` / throw a generic error) — NO real host detection, NO real install, NO live colima/docker/socket touch (never call the default `DependencyManager()` or `dockerDaemonResponds`).   - **Install edge cases (`DependencyInstallOutcomeTests`, Requirements 10.3/10.4/10.5):** over all six tools — cancelled-before-start ⇒ `.cancelled` + `runCount==0` (no side effect); offline ⇒ `.offline` + `runCount==0` (no doomed download); permission-denied ⇒ `.permissionDenied` with remediation (mentions the tool + "permission") + `runCount==1`; installer `.failed("…")` ⇒ `.failed` with the exact message; success (probe re-observes present+current) ⇒ `.installed`; + the distinct catch/branch arms (installer-thrown `.cancelled`/`.offline`, generic error → `.failed`, success-but-still-missing → `.failed`).

### 2026-07-23T14:30Z · swift-test-engineer (cross-frontend lane → scripts/**) · R7 task 12.4 — enforce cross-frontend accessibility-identifier uniqueness in hardening tests
- **intent:** Close design **Property 17** (accessibility-identifier totality + uniqueness — Requirements 5.7, 11.4) as a CROSS-FRONTEND hardening gate. Per-frontend Property-17 coverage already exists (macOS `AccessibilityIdentifierInventoryPropertyTests` task 8.3; Windows `AutomationIdInventoryTests` task 5.5; Linux GTK `set_widget_name`/AT-SPI task 6.x; TUI deterministic `Tabs`). This task adds the checker that enforces the property ACROSS all four frontends at once: (a) TOTALITY — every frontend exposes a non-empty, no-empty-string identifier inventory with per-frontend count floors (anti-vacuous); (b) WITHIN-SURFACE UNIQUENESS — for every frontend and every surface (file), no accessibility identifier is attached to two different controls (branch-reuse allowlisted, mirroring task 8.3); (c) CROSS-FRONTEND CANONICAL-SURFACE CONSISTENCY — the 12 core canonical surfaces each carry a navigation/surface identifier on EVERY frontend (each frontend covers the same shared surface set, in its platform-idiomatic scheme); (d) SHARED-ID NON-COLLISION — any surface-key identifier literal present in >1 frontend must denote the SAME canonical surface everywhere (e.g. `view_runtime` is macOS+Linux, both = runtime — the intentional cross-platform key recorded in the task-7.6 ledger entry), never two different surfaces.
- **why scripts/** (deviation from the task's nominal `Tests/**` owner, justified):** "cross-frontend" is inherently polyglot — a Swift XCTest target can only compile+scan Swift; it cannot read the Windows XAML (C#), Linux GTK (Rust), or TUI (Go) trees. A standalone scanner mirrors how `scripts/gen-truth-table.py` already scans ALL frontends, is invocable by `verify.sh`/the RC gate (task 12.5), and — critically — avoids adding a file to the macOS `Sources`+`Tests` build (the documented Xcode concurrent-compile hazard). It does NOT modify any frontend source or per-frontend test target; it only READS them.
- **definition of "cross-frontend uniqueness" (from design.md Property 17 + Requirement 11.4):** totality (non-empty id per interactive control) + per-surface uniqueness (no id collides with another control on the same surface) generalized to hold for ALL Desktop_Frontends, PLUS the cross-frontend consistency/non-collision of the shared canonical navigation surface keys. Naming schemes are deliberately platform-idiomatic and differ (macOS `tab_*`/`view_*`, Windows `Nav*` AutomationId, Linux `view_*` widget_name, TUI `Tabs` names) — so this is SEMANTIC surface-key consistency, never byte-identical id strings across frontends (requiring the latter would be a false violation).
- **plan (strictly inside `scripts/**` + one Makefile target):** (1) new standalone scanner `scripts/check-a11y-ids.py` — pure extractor + checker functions (Swift `.accessibilityIdentifier("…")` + `NavigationItem` reconstruction of `tab_*`; XAML `AutomationProperties.AutomationId`; Rust `.set_widget_name("…")`; Go `Tabs`) + a CLI that scans the real trees, prints per-frontend counts + surface coverage, and exits non-zero on any violation or vacuous (floor-not-met) result; excludes build output (`.build`/`bin`/`obj`/`target`). (2) `scripts/tests/test_check_a11y_ids.py` — the Property-17 property test: ≥100 randomized iterations validating the scanner detects duplicates/empties/missing-surface with no false positives on clean multi-frontend source, plus real-repo anchors; tagged `Feature: cross-platform-live-verification, Property 17`; mirrors the `test_gen_truth_table.py` importlib+pytest+CLI shape. (3) `Makefile` target `check-a11y` (`python3 scripts/check-a11y-ids.py`). Measured real floors (this host): macOS 366 ids/41 view files, Windows 300 ids/16 XAML, Linux 71 widget-names/13 view-roots, TUI 12 tabs.
- **files-to-touch:** `scripts/check-a11y-ids.py` (new, +x), `scripts/tests/test_check_a11y_ids.py` (new), `Makefile` (`check-a11y` target) + these append-only ledger entries. NOT touching `Sources/**`, `Tests/**`, `daemon/**`, `tui/**`, `windows/**`, `linux/**`, `proto/**`, `scripts/verify.sh` (task 12.1's lane), `.github/**` (task 12.5's lane), `docs/parity-matrix.md`, `docs/truth-table.csv`, or other board files.
- **outcome:** pending.
- **contract-impact:** none anticipated; read-only cross-frontend verification tooling. Consumes the frozen v1.1 contract + existing per-frontend identifier schemes unchanged (no RPC name/count/field-number touched). Maps Requirements 5.7, 11.4 → design Property 17 (accessibility identifier totality + uniqueness), generalized cross-frontend.

  - **"cross-frontend accessibility-identifier uniqueness" defined (design Property 17 + Requirement 11.4), enforced as four sub-checks:**
    - **A · Totality** — every frontend exposes a non-empty identifier inventory, ZERO empty-string ids, and meets per-frontend + global count floors (anti-vacuous).
    - **B · Within-surface uniqueness** — for every frontend and every surface (file), no identifier is attached to two different controls (branch-reuse allowlisted, mirroring the macOS task-8.3 allowlist `main_split_view`/`label_template_validation`).
    - **C · Cross-frontend canonical-surface consistency** — each of the **12 core canonical surfaces** (dashboard, containers, images, volumes, networks, configuration, profiles, kubernetes, ai, monitoring, machines, runtime) carries a navigation/surface identifier on EVERY frontend, in that frontend's idiomatic scheme (macOS `tab_*` computed from `NavigationItem`, Windows `Nav*` AutomationId, Linux `view_*` widget_name, TUI `Tabs`). This is semantic surface-key consistency — NOT byte-identical id strings (schemes deliberately differ per platform; requiring identical strings would be a false violation).
    - **D · Shared-id non-collision** — any surface-key literal present in >1 frontend must denote the SAME canonical surface everywhere. Exactly one is shared on the real trees: `view_runtime` (macOS ScrollView anchor + Linux surface root), both = the `runtime` surface — the intentional cross-platform key from the task-7.6 ledger entry. A shared key mapping to two surfaces is reported as a real collision (finding, never a fake pass).

    - **macOS**   — **292** literal `.accessibilityIdentifier(...)` across **33** surfaces; **13** navigation surface keys (12 core + `community`); 0 empty. (Dynamic/interpolated ids like `btn_use_profile_\(name)` are correctly excluded from the literal count, matching the task-8.3 scanner.)
    - **Windows** — **300** `AutomationProperties.AutomationId` across **14** XAML surfaces; **13** nav keys (12 core + `settings`); 0 empty.
    - **Linux**   — **71** `.set_widget_name("…")` literals across **15** Rust surfaces; **13** `view_*` surface roots (12 core + `onboarding`); 0 empty.
    - **TUI**     — **12** `Tabs` surface entries (exactly the 12 core); 0 empty.
    - Total ≈ **675** identifiers scanned; every frontend covers all 12 core canonical surfaces; the single shared surface-key (`view_runtime`) is consistent (macOS=runtime, Linux=runtime).
  - **verification (host-loaded):**
    - `python3 scripts/check-a11y-ids.py` → `RESULT: GREEN`, **exit 0** (evidence `/tmp/a11y_run2.txt`); `make check-a11y` (new target, `--report`) → GREEN, exit 0 (`/tmp/a11y_make2.txt`).

Recorded prominently in `docs/sbom/VULNERABILITY-REPORT.md` (Remediation guidance) for the owning agents.
  - **Reproducibility + gate wiring:** `make sbom` (SBOMs only), `make security-scan` (SBOMs + scanners + report), `make security-gate` (= `scripts/security-scan.sh --fail-on-critical`, exits non-zero on any critical → Requirement 11.3). `.github/workflows/security-scan.yml` (new, YAML-validated) runs the scan on push/PR/weekly-cron/dispatch, uploads `docs/sbom/` as an artifact, and enforces the zero-critical gate — the supply-chain gate the RC flow (task 12.5) and `release.yml` (task 13.1, "artifacts + SBOMs") consume.
  - **Verification:** real scanners executed with output captured to `docs/sbom/scans/`; SBOM byte-identical on regenerate; `scripts/tests/test_vuln_report.py` **RESULT: ALL PASS** (parser clean/vulnerable/not-run cases for govulncheck/cargo-audit/dotnet + a ≥150-iteration randomized gate property: verdict GREEN iff zero critical AND `--fail-on-critical` exits 2 iff critical>0). The tests caught + I fixed a REAL severity-classification bug: `_severity_from_cvss` was reading the "3.1" version out of a RustSec **CVSS vector** (which carries no base score) — corrected to map only a plain numeric score and label bare vectors `unspecified` (so a future critical Rust CVE is never silently under-scored; it is flagged unspecified for manual triage, documented in the report Notes). `bash -n` + `py_compile` clean; `make -n` targets parse.
- **contract-impact:** none. Ops/CI tooling + committed `docs/sbom/` evidence only; the frozen v1.1 proto and every component manifest were consumed READ-ONLY (no RPC name/count/field-number touched, no dependency version bumped). ### 2026-07-23T15:55Z · devops · R8 task 13.1 — build signed versioned artifacts on tag

- **intent:** Implement the R8 release pipeline that, when a version tag `vX.Y.Z` is pushed, builds VERSIONED, packaged artifacts for EVERY shipped component (macOS `.app`/`.dmg` + the Go daemon + the TUI; the Windows package on the `windows-winui` runner; the Linux package on the `linux-gtk4` runner) and signs them — with signing CREDENTIAL-GATED so it works in CI when secrets are present and degrades HONESTLY when they are absent (build the UNSIGNED artifact, label it `UNSIGNED — signing credential <NAME> absent`, never fake a signature, never commit a key, gate the notarize/publish step on credential presence). Emit a SHA-256 checksums manifest for every artifact. Wire `.github/workflows/release.yml` (tag-triggered) to DEPEND on the release-candidate gate (`.github/workflows/release-candidate.yml`, task 12.5) being GREEN, plus a local `scripts/package.sh` / `make package-dmg` path for the macOS artifact. Requirement 12.1.
- **version-stamping mechanism (single source of truth):** `scripts/version.sh` derives the version from the git tag (`vX.Y.Z` → marketing `X.Y.Z`; build number = `git rev-list --count HEAD`); extend it with machine-readable subcommands (`marketing`/`build`/`tag`/`json`) so every language's packaging reads ONE authority. Stamp per component WITHOUT editing owned source (prefer build-time injection): macOS Info.plist `CFBundleShortVersionString`/`CFBundleVersion` via the `MARKETING_VERSION`/`CURRENT_PROJECT_VERSION` xcodebuild build settings (project.yml has `GENERATE_INFOPLIST_FILE: true`); Go daemon+TUI via `-ldflags "-X main.version=<v> -X main.commit=<sha> -X main.date=<iso>"` (ready mechanism — targets the conventional `main.version` symbol; goes live the moment the daemon/tui owner adds a one-line `var version string` + a `--version`/`version` print, a minimal coordination point, NOT faked here); Windows product version via `dotnet publish -p:Version=<v> -p:FileVersion=<v> -p:AssemblyVersion=<v>` (no csproj edit); Linux package version stamped into the package name + a bundled `VERSION`/metadata (and a transient CI-only Cargo package-version set) — no committed `linux/**` source edit.
- **required signing credentials (names, for a fully-signed release):** macOS Developer ID app signing → `MACOS_CERTIFICATE_P12`, `MACOS_CERTIFICATE_PASSWORD`, `MACOS_SIGN_IDENTITY`, `KEYCHAIN_PASSWORD`; macOS notarization → `NOTARY_APPLE_ID`, `NOTARY_TEAM_ID`, `NOTARY_PASSWORD`; Sparkle auto-update signing → `SPARKLE_PRIVATE_KEY` (Ed25519); Windows Authenticode → `WINDOWS_CERTIFICATE_PFX`, `WINDOWS_CERTIFICATE_PASSWORD` (+ optional `WINDOWS_TIMESTAMP_URL`); Linux package signing → `LINUX_GPG_PRIVATE_KEY`, `LINUX_GPG_PASSPHRASE` (+ optional `LINUX_GPG_KEY_ID`). When a credential set is ABSENT the corresponding artifact is built UNSIGNED and labelled `UNSIGNED — signing credential <NAME> absent` in the build output AND the manifest `signing_status` field; notarize/publish steps SKIP with a clear message (never error-fake). The 13.2/13.3 tasks configure the actual signing keys; 13.1 makes the pipeline correct and READY to sign the moment secrets are set.
- **plan (strictly inside the devops lane):** (1) extend `scripts/version.sh` (machine-readable subcommands; keep the KEY=VALUE default for `package.sh`/`github-release.sh` backward-compat). (2) new `scripts/release/checksums.sh` — SHA-256 manifest generator: scans a dist dir, computes `sha256`+size per artifact, merges each artifact's `<artifact>.meta.json` sidecar (component/signed/signing_status/required_credentials), emits `SHA256SUMS.txt` + `release-manifest.json` (per-artifact + a signing summary + the aggregate required-credential name list). (3) new `scripts/package-go.sh` — build daemon/tui version-stamped via ldflags for a given GOOS/GOARCH (or macOS universal via lipo), credential-gated codesign on macOS, sidecar meta. (4) extend `scripts/package.sh` — parameterize `DERIVED_DATA`/`DIST_DIR`; after the `.app`/`.dmg`, also build the daemon universal binary and emit the checksums manifest; write the dmg sidecar meta with the honest signed/unsigned label. ### 2026-07-23T16:10Z · devops · R8 task 13.4 — implement the P0/P1 release-blocking gate

- **intent:** Implement the gate that BLOCKS a v1.0.0 release when any OPEN defect carries priority P0 (critical) or P1 (high) — Requirement 12.5, design Property 22 ("block the tag IFF the open-defect set contains at least one P0/P1"). COMPLEMENTARY to the release-candidate gate (task 12.5): the RC gate enforces build/test/coverage/security/perf/a11y GREEN; THIS gate enforces "no open P0/P1 defect"; BOTH must pass to ship (task 13.6). Define a clear machine-readable defect source, seed it honestly from the current board/STATUS/vuln state, wire it into the Makefile + the RC/release CI flow, and prove it actually blocks.
- **plan:** (1) create the curated machine-readable registry `docs/release-defects.json` (schema: each defect {id,title,priority,status,...}; block rule DERIVED from priority+status, never a hand-set boolean) seeded with the current known state; (2) implement the checker `scripts/release_blocking_gate.py` (pure importable functions + CLI, so task 13.5's Property-22 test can drive it) that reads the registry AND auto-ingests `docs/sbom/vuln-summary.json` (critical→P0, high→P1), exiting 0=pass / 1=blocked / 2=fail-closed; (3) a thin `scripts/release-blocking-gate.sh` wrapper (mirrors sbom.sh→sbom_gen.py); (4) `make release-gate`/`release-defects`; (5) CI: a `defect-gate` job in `release-candidate.yml` feeding the `rc-gate` verdict + a gate step at the top of `release.yml`'s publish job (task 13.6 consumes the same gate). Only invoke/reference the 12.x scripts; never edit verify.sh/rc-gate.sh/security-scan.sh/perf-budgets.sh/check-a11y-ids.py.

NOT touching `daemon/`, `tui/`, `windows/`, `linux/`, `Sources/`, `Tests/`, `proto/`, the 12.x-owned scripts, or reserved board/docs files.
- **defect source / format:** `docs/release-defects.json` — an object with a `defects` list (a bare list is also accepted). Each defect carries `{id, title, priority, status}` (+ optional severity/source/component/note). Priority normalizes case-insensitively (critical/blocker→P0, high→P1, medium/moderate→P2, low/unspecified/none→P3); a defect is OPEN unless its status is a known closed status (resolved/closed/fixed/wontfix/deferred/duplicate/invalid/not_applicable/verified), and unknown/missing status ⇒ OPEN (fail-closed). **Block rule: a defect blocks IFF normalized priority ∈ {P0,P1} AND status is open.** The gate also auto-ingests the task-12.2 `docs/sbom/vuln-summary.json` (critical→synthetic open P0, high→synthetic open P1) so a future critical/high CVE auto-blocks even if nobody hand-adds it.
- **honest seed (current known state):** 2 registry defects, classified honestly — (a) `VULN-2026-GOVULN-UNSPEC` **P3**: the 10 govulncheck findings from task 12.2 are ALL unspecified severity (critical=0, high=0), triage-only per Requirement 11.3 → NOT P0/P1, does not block; (b) `STATUS-2026-07-20-MACOS-VERIFY` **P2**: the macOS unit+integration FAIL recorded in STATUS.md (2026-07-20 verify.sh under the live desktop-e2e lane) — recorded so the gate does not hide a known-red signal, but classified P2 (not P0/P1) because it is INDEPENDENTLY enforced by the complementary RC gate's verify.sh-GREEN requirement (so the release still cannot ship while it is red), it is diagnosis-pending, and the prior green run predates current changes (possible live-lane/timing artifact). If triage confirms a product regression it is raised to P1 and this gate blocks.
- **current real P0/P1 count + verdict:** **P0=0, P1=0** open (open-by-priority: P0=0 P1=0 P2=1 P3=1) → **`RESULT: RELEASE-BLOCKING GATE GREEN`, exit 0** — the gate does not block the release on defects (the app is otherwise gated by the RC gate). Also verified via the auto-ingest path: `vuln-summary.json` critical=0/high=0 contributes 0 blocking defects.
- **block-on-synthetic proof (host-loaded → backgrounded to /tmp per the flaky login-shell hook; the committed registry was NEVER mutated — proofs used temp registries via `--registry`):**
  - baseline real registry → exit **0** (control);
  - inject synthetic OPEN P0 (`--registry` temp) → **exit 1**, prints `BLOCKING defects: [P0] SYN-P0 …` + `RESULT: RELEASE BLOCKED — 1 open P0/P1 defect(s)`;
  - inject synthetic OPEN P1 (`high`/`new`) → **exit 1**;
  - same P0 but `status:resolved` → **exit 0** (only OPEN P0/P1 blocks);
  - auto-ingest a synthetic CRITICAL in a temp vuln-summary → **exit 1** (`VULN-AUTO-CRITICAL` P0);
  - missing/unreadable registry → **exit 2** (fail-closed, `RESULT: RELEASE BLOCKED (defect source unreadable)`) — never a silent pass;
  - remove synthetic P0 → real registry passes again (**exit 0**); committed `docs/release-defects.json` confirmed unchanged (ids = [VULN-2026-GOVULN-UNSPEC, STATUS-2026-07-20-MACOS-VERIFY], no `SYN-*` leaked).
  - `release-blocking-gate.sh --selftest` → 11/11 block-logic cases flip correctly (empty/P2/P3/resolved/closed → no block; open P0/P1, synonyms, missing-status → block) → `selftest PASS`, exit 0.
- **wiring verified:** `make -n release-gate`/`release-defects` parse → `bash scripts/release-blocking-gate.sh [--list]`. `release.yml` (publish job): the "Release-blocking P0/P1 defect gate" step runs `bash scripts/release-blocking-gate.sh` FIRST (before Install XcodeGen / any artifact build), so a non-zero exit aborts the publish (task 13.6 consumes the same gate). `release-candidate.yml`: new `defect-gate` job runs the gate and is added to `rc-gate`'s `needs` + the aggregated verdict table (`P0/P1 defect gate:$R_DEFECTS`), so an RC cannot be GREEN with an open P0/P1. `frontends.yml`/`test.yml`/`security-scan.yml` re-validated as still well-formed YAML.

INVOKE (never edit) `scripts/release-blocking-gate.sh` (13.4), `scripts/release/checksums.sh`/`scripts/sbom.sh`/`scripts/release/summary.py` (13.1), and `release-candidate.yml` (12.5). - **outcome:** DONE — RESULT: **GREEN**. `release.yml` publish is now provably ALL-GREEN gated and YAML-valid; the invoked P0/P1 gate runs clean.
  - **exact gating chain (what the publish `needs:` + where the P0/P1 gate runs):** `release` (publish) `needs: [rc-gate, release-gate, macos, windows, linux]`. `rc-gate` = `uses: ./.github/workflows/release-candidate.yml` (task 12.5 reusable: 9 `frontends.yml` jobs + `test.yml` + `verify.sh` + security-scan + perf + a11y + its own `defect-gate`, aggregated by the RC workflow's terminal `rc-gate` job which `if: always()` + `exit 1` on any red gate). `release-gate` = a NEW explicit job (`needs: [rc-gate]`, ubuntu-latest) running `bash scripts/release-blocking-gate.sh` (task 13.4 P0/P1 gate). `macos`/`windows`/`linux` each `needs: [rc-gate]`. So the P0/P1 gate runs in **three** places relative to publish: (a) inside the RC gate's `defect-gate` job (transitive), (b) the dedicated `release-gate` job the publish directly `needs:`, and (c) re-asserted as the FIRST step of the publish job itself (`Re-assert P0/P1 release-blocking gate before publishing`, before the download/SBOM/checksums/gh-release/appcast steps).
  - **a red RC/gate/build blocks publish (confirmed):** the publish job carries NO `if:` (no `always()`/`!cancelled()` override), so GitHub Actions' default `needs:`-success semantics apply — if `rc-gate` (⇒ any of the 9 frontends jobs / test.yml / verify.sh / security / perf / a11y / RC defect-gate) fails, OR `release-gate` (open P0/P1 ⇒ exit 1, or unreadable defect source ⇒ exit 2) fails, OR any of `macos`/`windows`/`linux` fails, then EVERY dependent job is skipped and the publish (GitHub Release create/upload + appcast) NEVER runs. The builds themselves `needs: [rc-gate]`, so a red RC gate also prevents any artifact from being built. No release notes/assets/checksums/appcast are produced for a red tag.
  - **checksums + SBOM attached, idempotent/re-runnable:** the publish job generates SBOMs (`scripts/sbom.sh` → `dist/…-sbom.tar.gz`), then the aggregate SHA-256 manifest (`scripts/release/checksums.sh` → `SHA256SUMS.txt` + `release-manifest.json`), and uploads every `dist/` file except `*.meta.json` (so `SHA256SUMS.txt`, `release-manifest.json`, and the SBOM archive are all attached). Idempotent: `gh release view` → `gh release upload --clobber` + `gh release edit --notes-file` when the release exists, else `gh release create`; the appcast is committed only if it changed (`git diff --cached --quiet`); a run can be re-driven for an existing tag via `workflow_dispatch` (`inputs.tag`).
  - **version-tag flow:** `on.push.tags: ['v*']` — publishing fires on a `vX.Y.Z` tag push (the tag push itself is the human release action); a red gate blocks that tag's release via the `needs:` chain above. (`workflow_dispatch` allows re-releasing an existing tag.)
  - **signing stays credential-gated:** macOS cert-import + notarize steps are `if: HAS_SIGNING`/`HAS_NOTARY`; the appcast step is `if: HAS_SPARKLE`; the GitHub Release create/upload step has NO `if:` — so an unsigned-but-labelled release still publishes when signing secrets are absent, and the publish is blocked ONLY by an RC/P0-P1/build failure, never by missing signing credentials.
  - **verification (host loaded — backgrounded to /tmp logs; the interactive shell prints a repo-scan-hook banner + a spurious "Exit Code: -1", so real results were captured to files and read back):**
    - `/tmp/verify_task136.py` → `/tmp/verify_task136.log`: **RESULT: GREEN, EXIT=0**. `yaml.safe_load(release.yml)` + `release-candidate.yml` both parse; jobs = `[rc-gate, release-gate, macos, windows, linux, release]`; publish `needs` = the 5 expected (explicit + transitive closure both verified); builds + release-gate all `needs: rc-gate`; NO needs-defeating `if:` on any gate/build/publish job; `release-gate` runs the gate script + publish re-asserts it (step 2) BEFORE the gh-release/appcast publish (step 7); RC workflow HAS a `defect-gate` running the gate script, its aggregator `needs: defect-gate` and hard-fails (`exit 1`) on any red gate; `v*` tag trigger present; macOS signing `if: HAS_SIGNING`, appcast `if: HAS_SPARKLE`, and the release create/upload is NOT signing-gated.
    - `bash scripts/release-blocking-gate.sh` → `/tmp/task136_gate.log`: **GATE_EXIT=0**, `RESULT: RELEASE-BLOCKING GATE GREEN — 0 open P0/P1 defects` (open by priority P0=0 P1=0 P2=1 P3=1) — the invoked gate is real and currently permits publishing.
  - **RESULT: GREEN** — the publish job is provably gated on RC (12.5) + P0/P1 (13.4) + all three builds, `release.yml` is valid YAML, a red RC/gate/build skips the publish, and signing remains credential-gated (unsigned-but-labelled publishes; red gates block).
- **files touched:** `.github/workflows/release.yml` (added the explicit `release-gate` P0/P1 job; publish `needs: [rc-gate, release-gate, macos, windows, linux]`; on-runner P0/P1 re-assert as the publish job's first step; refreshed the pipeline-shape header + release-summary to document the all-green invariant) + these two append-only ledger entries. NOT touched: `scripts/release-blocking-gate.sh`/`scripts/release_blocking_gate.py` (13.4 — invoked only), `scripts/release/checksums.sh`/`scripts/sbom.sh`/`scripts/release/summary.py`/`scripts/package.sh` (13.1 — invoked only), the 12.x scripts, `release-candidate.yml` (12.5 — reused, not edited), `daemon/`, `tui/`, `windows/`, `linux/`, `Sources/`, `Tests/`, `proto/`, reserved docs, other board files.

- **intent:** Implement the notification secret-hygiene scrub so no credential/secret substring can reach a rendered message, an emitted log line, or a committed evidence artifact before write/transmit (design Property 25; Requirements 8.6 + 13.6).
- **plan:** Add a standalone, dependency-free `scripts/notify/scrub.py` exposing `scrub(text, known_secrets=()) -> str` (the redactor applied before write/transmit) and `assert_clean(text, known_secrets=()) -> None` (the design's "assert no secret substring present" tripwire), plus `find_secrets`/`contains_secret`/`scrub_result`. Define all types LOCALLY (no shared-file edit) so the concurrently-running 14.1/14.2 stay conflict-free.
- **files-to-touch:** `scripts/notify/scrub.py` (new; my lane only). NOT touched: `scripts/notify/render.py` (owned by 14.1), any deliver/channel-delivery file (14.2), any test file (14.4).
- **outcome:** DONE. Created `scripts/notify/scrub.py` (stdlib-only, import-safe, idempotent; matches `render.py` conventions — `%`-formatting, dataclasses, `--selftest`, no `from __future__`). Redacts to the stable placeholder `***REDACTED***`: PEM private-key blocks, JWTs, URL-embedded credentials (SMTP/postgres/https `scheme://user:PASS@host` — keeps structure, redacts the password), Authorization/Bearer headers (keeps the header + scheme word, redacts the credential), AWS/GitHub/Slack/SendGrid/Google/Stripe/Twilio token shapes, secret-like AND generic high-entropy `KEY=VALUE`/`KEY: VALUE` assignments (keeps the key, redacts the value; URLs/versions excluded from the entropy path), and every declared `known_secret` (full removal, longest-first). Findings and the `SecretLeakError` message are LOG-SAFE by construction (category + length + sha256[:12] fingerprint, never the raw secret) — satisfies the "…and from logs" half of R13.6. Verified with Python 3.14.4: `python3 -m py_compile scripts/notify/scrub.py` = COMPILE_OK; `--selftest` = PASS (18 invariant checks incl. idempotence `scrub(scrub(x))==scrub(x)`, completeness `assert_clean(scrub(x))` never raises, placeholder-not-flagged, and no over-redaction of version/release-URL); `--demo` = PASS (10 synthetic secrets redacted while the version/roadmap/verify-line/release-URL/`Authorization: Bearer`/`postgres://admin:` structure and a short non-secret token all survive). NOTE: only SYNTHETIC/example secret patterns were used anywhere (no real credentials in code or fixtures).
- **contract-impact:** none to the frozen gRPC contract. New scrub contract for 14.2/14.4 to consume — `scrub(text, known_secrets=()) -> str` (total for str input, idempotent, preserves non-secret content/structure) and `assert_clean(text, known_secrets=()) -> None` (raises `SecretLeakError` with a log-safe message when any secret remains); also `find_secrets(text, known_secrets=()) -> [Finding]`, `contains_secret(...) -> bool`, `scrub_result(...) -> ScrubResult`, the module constant `REDACTION_PLACEHOLDER`, and local types `Finding`/`ScrubResult`/`SecretLeakError`. Task 14.2 (channel delivery) will reconcile these locally-defined types into the shared delivery path and apply `scrub`/`assert_clean` before any log line, draft-to-file (`dist/notifications/*.txt`), or transmission; task 14.4 will drive `scrub`/`assert_clean` for the Property-25 test.

Since no SMTP/Twilio creds are configured in this environment, the DRAFT path is the primary exercised path today.
- **plan:** New Python module `scripts/notify/deliver.py` matching the `scripts/**` house style (module docstring with requirement/design refs; pure import-safe functions + thin argparse CLI; `main(argv)->int`; no `from __future__ import annotations` so it loads by importlib path). IMPORT the completed sibling modules unchanged (`render.py` 14.1 + `scrub.py` 14.3) by putting `scripts/notify` on `sys.path` (their names have no hyphen). Reconcile render's local `EmailPayload`/`SmsPayload` into a single shared delivery contract (`OutboundMessage`) here (the reconciliation the 14.1 owner noted 14.2 performs). Build the v1.0.0 summary via `render.default_v1_summary()` + `render.render(summary)` (the facts are already encoded in render's defaults). Credential detection is env-only + pure; the real send paths are stdlib-only (smtplib/urllib, lazily imported) and credential-gated; drafting scrubs + asserts clean before writing; a send failure falls back to a draft so the release is never blocked.

Did NOT write any test file (task 14.4 owns those). Generated output `dist/notifications/**` is git-ignored via the repo-root `/dist/` rule (confirmed) — never committed. Plus this append-only ledger entry.
- **delivery contract (for 14.4 to test against + the release step to call):**
  - **Types (reconcile render's payloads into the shared delivery unit):** `OutboundMessage{channel, recipient, subject, body}` (secret-free; `message_from_email`/`message_from_sms`/`message_for_optional` adapt render's `EmailPayload`/`SmsPayload`); `ChannelCredentials{channel, provider, complete, present_vars, missing_vars, optional}` (records env-var NAMES only, never values); `DeliveryResult{channel, status: sent|drafted|skipped, provider, recipient, draft_path, missing_vars, detail}`.
  - **Public functions:** `load_creds(channel, env=os.environ) -> ChannelCredentials` (+ `load_email_creds`/`load_sms_creds`); `deliver(channel, message, env, *, version, out_dir, known_secrets, force_draft, log) -> DeliveryResult` (the design entry point); `deliver_all(rendered, env, *, version, out_dir, requested, force_draft, ...) -> [DeliveryResult]`; `write_draft`/`ensure_send_script`/`write_combined_json`; `known_secret_values(env)`; `main(argv)->int`.
  - **Env vars each channel requires (env-var NAMES only; read from the ENVIRONMENT, never hardcoded):** email → `SENDGRID_API_KEY` (SendGrid) OR `SMTP_HOST`+`SMTP_USER`+`SMTP_PASS` (+ optional `SMTP_PORT`,`SMTP_FROM`) (SMTP); sms → `TWILIO_ACCOUNT_SID`+`TWILIO_AUTH_TOKEN`+`TWILIO_FROM` (Twilio/equivalent); optional whatsapp → `TWILIO_ACCOUNT_SID`+`TWILIO_AUTH_TOKEN`+`TWILIO_WHATSAPP_FROM`; optional telegram → `TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN`+`TELEGRAM_CHAT_ID`; optional viber → `VIBER_AUTH_TOKEN`+`VIBER_RECEIVER`.   - **Draft-fallback (Property 24 / R13.4 — the primary path today):** required channels (email, sms) whose creds are absent/partial → DRAFT to `dist/notifications/<channel>-<version>.txt` (e.g. `email-v1.0.0.txt`, `sms-v1.0.0.txt`) + a combined `notifications-v1.0.0.json` + a re-runnable `dist/notifications/send-notifications.sh` (re-invokes `deliver.py` once creds are exported; contains NO credential — env-var NAMES only). Optional channels attempted only WHERE configured (R13.5), else `skipped` (not drafted). Real-send failure → draft fallback (release never fails). `deliver`/`deliver_all`/`main` return success (exit 0) when channels are sent/drafted/skipped — a drafted channel does NOT fail the release.

A `SecretLeakError` is fail-closed (exit 2, nothing written/transmitted).
